<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> At the prime three and height three, the chromatic overlap cannot be constructed from the rational, height-one, and height-two local spheres by finitely many sums, shifts, cofibers, and retracts in the category of $`E(2)`$-local modules over the derived 3-complete sphere. This gives a negative answer to the ordinary finite-assembly question for chromatic overlaps.

- **claim-finite-assembly-obstruction** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): At $p=n=3$, with $S=S_3^\wedge$, $$L_2L_{K(3)}S \notin\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}\{L_0S,L_1S,L_2S\}$$ in the category of $E(2)$-local $S$-modules.
  - Explanation: The chromatic overlap at the specified prime and height cannot be obtained from the lower local spheres by the ordinary finite closure operations.
- **claim-mod-p-nullity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mod-p-nullity)): Multiplication by $p$ is null as an $E_2$-module map on $E_2/p$. For every spectrum $X$, it therefore annihilates the homotopy groups of $L_{K(2)}(E_2\wedge X)/p$. Those groups are naturally $\mathcal O_x$-modules.
  - Explanation: Multiplication by the prime is null on the reduced module spectrum and annihilates the tested homotopy groups; the homotopy groups themselves need not vanish.
- **claim-constituents-forced-by-assembly** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-constituent-test)): Suppose that $L_{K(2)}X$ belongs to the ordinary thick subcategory generated by $\mathbf 1_2$ in $K(2)$-local spectra. Then, for every $d\in\mathbb Z$, the representation $\mathcal V_d(X)$ is finite-dimensional over $Q_x$, and each simple constituent is isomorphic to $\overline\omega^{\otimes b}$ for some $b\in\mathbb Z$.
  - Explanation: Ordinary thick generation forces both finite dimensionality and periodicity-power simple constituents in every tested degree.
- **claim-finite-p-basis** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-p-basis)): The ordinary module $\Omega^1_{A/k}$ is free on $da,dt$ and agrees with the module of continuous differentials. The element $t$ is a $p$-basis of $K$, remains a $p$-basis at each finite separable field extension of $K$, and gives $\Omega^1_{L/k}=L\,dt$ for the marking algebra defined below.
  - Explanation: The deformation parameters freely generate ordinary differentials, and the Laurent parameter remains a differential basis throughout the separable marking tower.
- **claim-primitive-torsion-coordinates** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-primitive-coordinates)): For $l\geq 1$, let $\mathcal B_l$ be the reduced closure, over $A_2$, of the points of $G[p^l]_K$ whose images generate the étale quotient. Write $s_l$ for its point coordinate and set $s_j=[p^{l-j}](s_l)$ for $1\leq j\leq l$. Then $$\mathcal B_l=k[[s_l]],\qquad \mathcal C_l:=k[[t]][s_l^{d^l}]=k[[s_l^{d^l}]]. \label{eq:primitive-rings}$$ The algebra $\mathcal C_l[1/t]$ is the finite étale algebra of generators of $G_K^{\mathrm{et}}[p^l]$. Over it, the algebra of *all* lifts of the universal generator is $\mathcal B_l[1/t]$, finite free of rank $d^l$. The transition maps are finite and injective, and $$s_j\in k[[s_l^{d^{l-j}}]]\qquad(l\geq j). \label{eq:primitive-transitions}$$
  - Explanation: The primitive closures have explicit power-series coordinates. Their Frobenius-divided coordinates classify étale generators, and the associated lift algebra retains every lift of that generator.
- **claim-universal-cover-coordinates** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cover-coordinates)): There is a natural additive isomorphism $$\Phi_t:\mathcal H_3(C,C^+)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \varprojlim_{[p]}G_t(C^{\circ\circ}).$$ Its coordinates and inverse are $$\Phi_t(z)_j=\lim_{r\longrightarrow\infty} P_t^{\circ r}\bigl(z^{1/q^{j+r}}\bigr), \qquad \Phi_t^{-1}((b_j))=\lim_{j\longrightarrow\infty}b_j^{q^j}. \label{eq:cover-limit}$$ The isomorphism is $\mathbb Q_p$-linear and is compatible with coefficient maps and changes of deformation frame. On an algebraically closed perfectoid field, the zeroth projection is surjective and its kernel is the integral Tate module of $G_t^{\mathrm{et}}$.
  - Explanation: Normalized iterates identify the Honda cover with the inverse multiplication tower, compatibly with frames and coefficients.
- **claim-generator-ball-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-generator-ball)): There is a natural $P\times\mathbb Z_p^\times$-equivariant isomorphism $$\mathcal T_{\mathrm{et}}\simeq\mathcal H_3\setminus\{0\}, \qquad (s_l)\longmapsto z=\lim_l s_l^{q^l}. \label{eq:generator-ball}$$ It identifies completed function algebras, with their spectral norms, on an exhaustion by smaller closed balls. The assertion commutes with base change and remains valid after adjoining any compact profinite parameter space.
  - Explanation: The complete integral generator tower is the punctured Honda cover, with compatible spectral norms and continuous profinite families.
- **claim-relative-section-full-faithfulness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-section-facts)): There are natural isomorphisms of additive sheaves $$\mathcal H_i\simeq \mathcal H^0(V_i),\qquad \mathcal H^0(\mathcal O)=\underline{\mathbb Q_p}.$$ The automorphism sheaf of $V_i$ is the locally profinite group $D_i^\times$. A family fiberwise isomorphic to $V_i$ is locally of that form on the perfectoid site. Trivial bundles and these positive basic bundles have no higher relative cohomology sheaves. For such bundles, $\underline{\mathbb Q_p}$-linear maps between the section sheaves are exactly the maps of bundles, relatively and compatibly with base change.
  - Explanation: Relative section sheaves identify the basic bundles and recover bundle morphisms, while the relevant higher relative cohomology sheaves vanish.
- **claim-stable-bundle-tests** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-stable-bundle-tests)): A section of $V_3$ nonzero on every geometric fiber gives a subbundle $\mathcal O\hookrightarrow V_3$ with quotient locally isomorphic to $V_2$. Conversely, an extension $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal O\longrightarrow E \longrightarrow V_2\longrightarrow0 \label{eq:basic-extension}$$ has middle bundle fiberwise $V_3$ if and only if its extension class is nonzero on every geometric fiber. Moreover, $\mathcal H^0(V_2^\vee)=0$.
  - Explanation: A fiberwise nonzero section has the required basic quotient, and a nowhere-split extension has the required basic middle bundle.
- **claim-integral-frame-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-integral-frames)): There exists a determinant identification $\iota:\det V_3\xrightarrow{\sim}\det V_2$ over $k$ for which the following conclusions hold. Fix such an identification for the sequel. There is an isomorphism $$\widetilde X\simeq\operatorname{Surj}(V_3,V_2). \label{eq:connected-surjections}$$ A surjection has a unique kernel frame for which its determinant identification is $\iota$. Under <a href="#eq:connected-surjections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:connected-surjections">[eq:connected-surjections]</a>, this is an integral étale Tate generator for the corresponding deformation. The source-frame group $P$ acts by reduced norm on this normalization, and a target-frame change in $J$ has its corresponding constant determinant adjustment.
  - Explanation: A determinant normalization identifies connected markings with bundle surjections and makes their kernel frames integral Tate generators.
- **claim-perfected-marking-torsor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-perfected-marking-torsor)): For each open $U\subset J$, the map $$\widetilde X\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Spa}}(B^\flat),\qquad B=L^U,$$ is a pro-étale $U$-torsor. It is natural in $U$, in coefficient base change, and in the commuting $P$-action. The space $\widetilde X$ is represented by the completed perfection of the whole algebraic marking tower, equipped with its uniform valuation norm; this representation is used here only to discuss its geometry.
  - Explanation: Each completed finite marking stage is the quotient of the perfected tower by its open marking subgroup; the whole completion is used for geometry.
- **claim-determinant-one-two-towers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-two-towers)): For each open $U\subset J$ and $B=L^U$ there is a natural equivalence of $v$-stacks over $k$ $$\simeq[N^*/U]. \label{eq:two-towers}$$ It is obtained from the commuting torsor presentations $$\widetilde X/U\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Spa}}(B^\flat),\qquad \widetilde X/H\simeq N^*.$$ The residual group $P/H=\mathbb Z_p^\times$ acts on $N^*$ by kernel scalars through reduced norm, up to the common inverse convention. The $J$-action changes the quotient frame and makes the kernel determinant adjustment in <a href="#eq:normalized-frame-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:normalized-frame-action">[eq:normalized-frame-action]</a>. All these identifications commute with base change and products with profinite parameter spaces.
  - Explanation: Commuting marking and determinant-one torsors compare finite-stage quotients with the negative period space, retaining both frame actions.
- **claim-finite-etale-section-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-etale-descent)): Let $L=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_i B_i$ be an injective union of finite étale $K$-algebras, with their maximum valuation norms, and let $C=\widehat{L^{\mathrm{perf}}}$. For every finite étale $L$-algebra $E$, the map $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{L\text{-alg}}(E,L)\longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{L\text{-alg}}(E,C)$$ is bijective. Each section on the right is defined at a finite separable stage. The assertion allows unbounded numbers of field factors among the $B_i$.
  - Explanation: Finite étale sections over the completed perfection descend uniquely to finite separable stages, even when the number of factors is unbounded.
- **claim-algebraic-etale-marking** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-etale-marking)): The étale quotient of $G_L$ has a compatible integral Tate generator defined over $L$, obtained by determinant normalization. It is fixed by $H$. Transport by $P$ changes it by the constant reduced-norm unit, up to the common inverse convention; transport by $J$ has the constant determinant adjustment determined by <a href="#eq:normalized-frame-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:normalized-frame-action">[eq:normalized-frame-action]</a>. Each finite torsion level of this marking is defined at a finite separable marking stage.
  - Explanation: The determinant-normalized integral generator descends to ordinary marking coefficients, with its norm and frame transformation laws.
- **claim-generator-lift-torsors** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-lift-torsors)): The scheme of lifts in $G_L[p^l]$ of the normalized étale generator has coordinate algebra $$R_l=L^{1/p^{2l}}=L^{1/d^l}\subset R=L^{\mathrm{perf}}. \label{eq:lift-rings}$$ It is a torsor under the marked group $\Gamma_2[p^l]_L$. The transition maps give the indicated inclusions inside $R$. The action of $H$ commutes with translation by this constant group. The other frame actions conjugate translations by continuous constant Honda automorphisms and scalar norm units. All coactions and their finite-level action identities are defined and continuous after passage to a sufficiently large finite separable stage.
  - Explanation: Lifts of the normalized generator form Honda torsors with the indicated purely inseparable coordinate algebras and compatible finite-stage actions.
- **claim-negative-period-presentation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-negative-presentation)): Let $C^\flat$ be an algebraically closed perfectoid field of characteristic $p$ containing $k$, choose an algebraically closed untilt $C/\mathbb C_p$, and let $F/\mathbb Q_p$ be unramified quadratic. After choosing its embedding at the untilt divisor there are isomorphisms $$N_{C^\flat}\simeq (\mathbb G_{a,C})^\diamond/\underline F, \qquad (N^*)_{C^\flat}\simeq [ (\mathbb A^1_C\setminus\underline F)^\diamond/ \underline F]. \label{eq:negative-presentation}$$ Here $\mathbb A^1_C$ is the analytic affine line. In the second expression the $F$-support is removed fiberwise before taking the translation quotient. The action of $F^\times$ through the quotient frame is multiplication in this presentation, up to inversion and the choice of unramified embedding. The additional kernel determinant adjustment from Theorem <a href="#thm:two-towers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:two-towers">13</a> is a scalar in $\mathbb Z_p^\times$.
  - Explanation: After choosing an untilt embedding, the negative space is an affine-line translation quotient, with the field support removed before forming the punctured quotient.
- **claim-finite-analytic-resolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-resolution)): Let $G$ be a compact $p$-adic analytic group with no element of order $p$, and put $d=\dim G$. The trivial module $\mathbb F_p$ admits a length-$d$ resolution $Q_\bullet$ by finitely generated projective $\mathbb F_p[[G]]$-modules, with their compact topologies. If $M$ is a complete linearly topologized $\mathbb F_p$-representation with invariant neighborhoods, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb F_p[[G]],\mathrm{cts}}(Q_\bullet,M) \simeq C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(G,M)$$ by continuous chain-homotopy equivalences. Each term on the left is a continuous direct summand of a finite power of $M$.* *For $G=H$ the dimension is eight, and the reversed group-ring dual of $Q_\bullet$ resolves the trivial right $\mathbb F_p[[H]]$-module. In particular, continuous $H$-cohomology of these coefficients vanishes above degree eight, and finite coefficient modules have finite cohomology.
  - Explanation: A compact analytic group without prime-order torsion has a finite projective resolution that computes continuous cochains on the stated complete coefficients.
- **claim-dense-contraction-roos-surjectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-analytic-roos)): Let $$M_0 \xleftarrow{f_0} M_1 \xleftarrow{f_1} M_2 \xleftarrow{} \cdots$$ be a countable inverse system of complete nonarchimedean normed abelian groups. Suppose every $f_n$ is a contraction with dense image. Then the map $$\prod_{n\geq0}M_n\longrightarrow\prod_{n\geq0}M_n,\qquad (x_n)\longmapsto(x_n-f_n(x_{n+1})) \label{eq:analytic-roos}$$ is surjective. The same assertion holds after replacing every $M_n$ by $\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,M_n)$. Consequently these systems have no positive derived inverse limits.
  - Explanation: Dense contracting transitions and completeness make the countable Roos difference map surjective, including continuous compact profinite families.
- **claim-annulus-restriction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-annulus-restriction)): The degree-zero cohomology of <a href="#eq:analytic-annulus-complex" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-annulus-complex">[eq:analytic-annulus-complex]</a> is $R_T$. Suppose $r\leq r'\leq b'\leq b$ and $$r'\geq r/\theta,\qquad b'\leq b\theta. \label{eq:analytic-radius-gap}$$ After restriction to $A[r',b']$, every degree-one class represented by a Laurent series with zero constant coefficient vanishes. Division by $\epsilon^u-1$, for $u\neq0$, has operator norm at most one between the indicated weighted coefficient spaces. The remaining degree-one class is the Kummer class of $z$, with coefficients in $R_T$.
  - Explanation: The prescribed radius gaps permit bounded division on nonconstant Laurent coefficients, killing their restricted cohomology classes while preserving the Kummer class.
- **claim-affine-line-acyclicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-line-flat)): For every compact profinite $T$, $$H^i_v\bigl(T\times(\mathbb A^1_C)^\diamond,\mathcal O^\flat\bigr) = \begin{cases} R_T,&i=0,\\ 0,&i>0. \end{cases} \label{eq:analytic-affine-line}$$ The identification in degree zero is induced by constants.
  - Explanation: The analytic affine line has only constant structure-sheaf cohomology, also with arbitrary compact profinite parameters.
- **claim-single-hole-residue** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-single-hole-residue)): The support complex $$\operatorname{fib}\left( R\Gamma_v(T\times\mathbb A^{1,\diamond}_C,\mathcal O^\flat) \longrightarrow R\Gamma_v(T\times E(c,r)^\diamond,\mathcal O^\flat)\right)$$ has cohomology only in degree $2$, where it is $Q(c,r;T)$. Constant-coefficient extraction induces a surjection $$\operatorname{res}_{c,r}:Q(c,r;T)\longrightarrow R_T. \label{eq:analytic-residue}$$ Its kernel maps to zero under restriction $Q(c,r;T)\to Q(c,r';T)$ whenever $r'\geq r/\theta$. Residues are preserved by enlarging a disc, by changing its center inside the enlarged disc, and by scalar coordinate changes.
  - Explanation: Single-hole support is represented by the exterior cokernel. Its residue is surjective, and enlarging the hole by the stated gap kills the residue kernel.
- **claim-field-support-distributions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-field-support)): For every compact profinite $T$, the complex $$\operatorname{fib}\left( R\Gamma_v(T\times\mathbb A^{1,\diamond}_C,\mathcal O^\flat) \longrightarrow R\Gamma_v(T\times (\mathbb A^1_C\setminus\underline F)^\diamond, \mathcal O^\flat)\right)$$ has cohomology only in degree $2$, where it is naturally $\mathcal D_{\mathrm{lf}}(F,R_T)$. This identification is equivariant for translations and scalar multiplication by $F^\times$, with its natural action on distributions.
  - Explanation: Support on the locally profinite field is the locally finite distribution module, naturally under translations and scalar multiplication.
- **claim-translation-cohomology-orientation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-translation-cohomology)): Let $R_0=C^\flat$ or $R_T$, with trivial additive $F$-action. Then $$H^i_{\mathrm{cts}}(F,R_0)= \begin{cases}R_0,&i=0,\\0,&i>0,\end{cases} \label{eq:analytic-constant-translations}$$ and $$H^i_{\mathrm{cts}}(F,\mathcal D_{\mathrm{lf}}(F,R_0))= \begin{cases}R_0,&i=2,\\0,&i\neq2.\end{cases} \label{eq:analytic-distribution-translations}$$ In <a href="#eq:analytic-distribution-translations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-distribution-translations">[eq:analytic-distribution-translations]</a>, multiplication by $u\in\mathcal O_F^\times$ acts by $N_{F/\mathbb Q_p}(u)^{-1}\bmod p$, under the convention of pushforward on distributions and conjugation on cochains.
  - Explanation: Additive-field cohomology distinguishes trivial coefficients from locally finite distributions; the latter retain the inverse determinant orientation of unit multiplication.
- **claim-geometric-period-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-geometric-cohomology)): For every compact profinite $T$, the geometric period domain has cohomology $$H^i_v(T\times(N^\ast)_{C^\flat},\mathcal O^\flat) = \begin{cases} R_T,&i=0,\\ R_T\otimes_{C^\flat}\ell_C,&i=3,\\ 0,&i\neq0,3, \end{cases} \label{eq:analytic-geometric-rows}$$ where $\ell_C$ is a one-dimensional $C^\flat$-space. All identifications are natural under continuous maps of $T$ and the frame-group actions. On $\ell_C$, $\mathcal O_F^\times\subset J$ acts through the nontrivial character $$\delta(u)=N_{F/\mathbb Q_p}(u)\bmod3.$$ The kernel-scalar action of $\mathbb Z_p^\times$ is trivial. The line spaces in <a href="#eq:analytic-geometric-rows" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-geometric-rows">[eq:analytic-geometric-rows]</a> have their usual topology, as expressed by the continuous-parameter formula.
  - Explanation: The geometric period domain has the constant line and an upper orientation line carrying the nontrivial residue norm character; kernel scalars act trivially.
- **claim-fixed-stage-frobenius-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frobenius-descent)): Let $B$ be a finite product of finite separable extensions of $k((t))$. For every compact profinite $Q$ there is an exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,B^\flat) \longrightarrow \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,\mathcal A_B) \xrightarrow{\Phi-1} \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,\mathcal A_B) \longrightarrow0. \label{eq:analytic-frobenius-exact}$$ It is natural in $B,Q$ and continuous actions over $k$. In particular, for a compact group $G$ acting continuously on $B$, over $k$ and preserving its valuations, the natural map identifies actual cochain complexes by $$C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(G,B^\flat;T) \simeq \operatorname{fib}\left( \Phi-1:C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(G,\mathcal A_B;T) \longrightarrow C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(G,\mathcal A_B;T)\right). \label{eq:analytic-frobenius-cochains}$$
  - Explanation: The Frobenius difference sequence is exact on continuous families and identifies arithmetic cochains with the fiber on geometric coefficients at a fixed finite stage.
- **claim-completed-stage-calculation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-completed-calculation)): For a cofinal family of sufficiently small open normal subgroups $U\subset J$, put $B=L^U$. Then $H^a_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\flat)$ is finite for every $a$, and it is zero for $a>7$. For every compact profinite $T$, evaluation induces the natural identification $$H^aC^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\flat;T) = \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}} \bigl(T,H^a_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\flat)\bigr), \label{eq:analytic-finite-stage-parameters}$$ where the finite group on the right is discrete. Moreover, $$(B^\flat)^H=k,\qquad L^H=k, \label{eq:analytic-invariant-field}$$ and $$\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_U H^aC^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^\flat;T) = \begin{cases} \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,\\ \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,\ell),&a=3,\\ 0,&a\neq0,3. \end{cases} \label{eq:analytic-completed-colimit}$$ The colimit identifications respect restrictions, conjugations and the $J$-action. The residual $P/H=\mathbb Z_p^\times$-action on both lines is trivial; on the upper line $\mathcal O_F^\times\subset J$ acts by $\delta$. The statements are natural in $T$.
  - Explanation: Small finite completed stages have finite cohomology and discrete parameter evaluation. Their marking colimit leaves the constant and norm-character lines.
- **claim-compact-discrete-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-duality)): Let $M$ be a compact continuous $\mathbb F_p$-representation of $H$ and give $$M^\vee=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb F_p,\mathrm{cts}}(M,\mathbb F_p)$$ the discrete topology and the contragredient action. Continuous $H$-cohomology of $M$ is compact Hausdorff in its ordinary cochain quotient topology, and there are natural isomorphisms $$\label{eq:compact-cohomology-duality} H^a_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,M)^\vee \cong H^{8-a}_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,M^\vee).$$ The transposed coefficient map induces the dual cohomology map. The reversed assertion holds for a discrete module and its compact dual.
  - Explanation: Compact coefficient cohomology is Hausdorff and dual to discrete coefficient cohomology in complementary degrees, naturally under coefficient maps.
- **claim-finite-cohomology-strictness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-strictness)): Let $A^\bullet$ be a complex of complete, separable, metrizable, linearly topologized $\mathbb F_p$-spaces with continuous linear differentials. If $H^a(A^\bullet)$ is finite, the boundary subgroup is open and closed in the cycle group, and the differential onto that boundary subgroup is open. Thus, for every neighborhood $V$ of zero in $A^{a-1}$, sufficiently small $a$-cocycles have primitives in $V$.
  - Explanation: Finite cohomology forces clopen boundaries and an open differential, so sufficiently small cocycles admit arbitrarily small primitives.
- **claim-profinite-parameter-lifting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-profinite-parameters)): Let $T$ be an arbitrary compact profinite space.* 1. *A continuous map from $T$ to $F$ lifts across an open continuous surjection $E\twoheadrightarrow F$ of complete metrizable linearly topologized groups.* 2. *If a complex $A^\bullet$ of complete, separable, metrizable, linearly topologized $\mathbb F_p$-spaces with continuous linear differentials has finite cohomology, then naturally $$\label{eq:finite-cohomology-parameters} H^a C_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,A^\bullet) \cong C_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,H^a(A^\bullet)),$$ where the group on the right is discrete.* 3. *For a filtered diagram of such complexes, this identity commutes with the cochain colimit, with the colimit of cohomology groups given the discrete topology.
  - Explanation: Open surjections lift continuous profinite families; finite cohomology therefore commutes with continuous parameters and the prescribed filtered cochain colimits.
- **claim-translation-distribution-operator** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-distribution-operator)): After the fixed finite enlargement of $k$, the inverse-limit distribution algebra of the translation tower is $k[[D]]$. A parameter $D$ can be chosen with the following properties.* 1. *Its action on $R$ is $L$-linear and locally nilpotent, commutes with $H$, and satisfies $$\label{eq:distribution-kernels} \ker(D^{p^i}:R\to R)=L^{1/p^i}\quad(i\ge0), \qquad D:R\twoheadrightarrow R.$$* 2. *The order-two norm quotient acts on $D$ by its nontrivial character. The action of $J$ on $k[[D]]$ is continuous on finite jets.* 3. *If $i$ is positive, even, and divisible by $[k:\mathbb F_p]$, and $q=p^i$, then $$\label{eq:distribution-frobenius} D(f^q)=(D^qf)^q,\qquad D^q(af)=aD^qf\quad(a\in L^{1/q}).$$* 4. *Each operator on a fixed root stage is continuous after passage to a finite larger marking stage. Every finite target stage has a continuous additive lift under $D$ at some finite larger marking and root stage. Thus the sequence with kernel $L$ and operator $D$ is exact on the prescribed cochains, also with profinite parameters.
  - Explanation: A translation distribution parameter detects root stages by its kernels, is surjective on the union, has the required sign action, and admits continuous finite-stage lifts.
- **claim-invariant-differential** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-invariant-differential)): There is an $H$-invariant basis $\eta\in\Omega^1_{L/k}$ defined, together with its inverse frame, at one finite separable marking stage.
  - Explanation: The marking algebra has an invariant differential basis whose frame and inverse already occur at a finite separable stage.
- **claim-cartier-traces-residue-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cartier-traces)): For every $i\ge1$, with $q=p^i$, there is $c_i\in k^\times$ such that $$\label{eq:Cartier-distribution-trace} D^{q-1}(f^{1/q})=c_i C_0^i(f)\qquad(f\in L).$$ In particular, $C_0(f^p)=0$. If $B=L^U$ contains $\eta$ as a basis, then $$\label{eq:Cartier-exact-sequence} 0\longrightarrow B\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Fr}}B \xrightarrow{d/\eta}B\xrightarrow{C_0}B\longrightarrow0$$ is exact and its two short exact factors have continuous additive sections onto their images.* *Write $B=\prod_j\kappa_j((s_j))$ and $\eta=h_j(s_j)\,ds_j/s_j$ on its factors. Define $$M=\{f:\operatorname{ord}_{s_j}(f_jh_j)>0\text{ for all }j\}, \qquad M^0=\{f:\operatorname{ord}_{s_j}(f_jh_j)\ge0\text{ for all }j\}.$$ These are compact open $H$- and $C_0$-stable lattices, and $M^0/M$ is finite. For the pairing $$\label{eq:residue-pairing} \langle f,g\rangle_B =\sum_j\operatorname{Tr}_{\kappa_j/\mathbb F_p} \operatorname{Res}_{s_j}(f_jg_j\eta)$$ there are topological $H$-equivariant identifications $$\label{eq:residue-lattice-duals} M^\vee=B/\mathcal O_B,\qquad (B/M)^\vee=\mathcal O_B,\qquad \langle C_0f,g\rangle_B=\langle f,g^p\rangle_B.$$
  - Explanation: Distribution traces are normalized Cartier iterates. Cartier exactness and residue pairings give the compact and discrete lattice duals used in finiteness.
- **claim-good-finite-stages** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-good-stages)): There is a cofinal collection of open normal uniform subgroups $U\subset J$ for which $B=L^U$ has all the following properties:* 1. *$\eta$ is a basis over $B$ and $H^a_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\flat)$ is finite for every $a$;* 2. *there is $\theta_0\in B^{1/p}$ with $D\theta_0=1$;* 3. *for every $\sigma\in U$, $$\label{eq:good-stage-jets} \sigma(D)-D\in D^{p+2}k[[D]].$$* *For these stages $D$ restricts to a continuous $H$-equivariant map $B^{1/p}\to B^{1/p}$.
  - Explanation: A cofinal normal uniform family contains the differential frame, a distribution preimage, and enough fixed distribution jets to preserve the first root stage.
- **claim-completed-positive-acyclicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-completed-positive)): At every good stage $B$, the complex $C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,(B^\flat)^{++})$ is acyclic. Moreover, $H^a(H,\mathcal O_{B^\flat})$ and $H^a(H,B^\flat/\mathcal O_{B^\flat})$ are finite for every $a$.
  - Explanation: At good stages, completed positive coefficients are acyclic, while integral coefficients and their principal-part quotients have finite cohomology.
- **claim-compact-operator-finiteness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-operator)): Let $N$ be a compact Hausdorff $\mathbb F_p$-vector space, let $C:N\to N$ be continuous and $\mathbb F_p$-linear, and suppose that $$I=\bigcap_{n\ge0}C^nN \quad\text{and}\quad N/CN$$ are finite. Then $\ker C$ is finite, and every subgroup of $N$ whose elements have finite forward $C$-orbits is finite. If $f:N\to X$ is a homomorphism with finite kernel, then $$\label{eq:stable-image-finite-fiber} \bigcap_{n\ge0}f(C^nN)=f(I).$$ No topology on $X$ is required in this last assertion.
  - Explanation: Finite stable image and finite cokernel force finite kernel and finite-orbit subgroups. A finite-kernel map commutes with the stable-image intersection.
- **claim-finite-root-stage-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-stage-finiteness)): For every good $B$, every finite root stage $B^{1/p^e}$, and every $H$-stable valuation-order lattice in such a stage, continuous $H$-cohomology is finite in every degree.
  - Explanation: Good separable stages, their finite root stages, and their invariant valuation-order lattices all have finite continuous cohomology.
- **claim-natural-decompletion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-decompletion)): For every compact profinite space $T$, the natural coefficient inclusions induce a quasi-isomorphism $$\label{eq:natural-decompletion} C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,R;T) =\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{U,e}C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^{1/p^e};T) \longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_UC^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^\flat;T).$$ It is natural in $T$, in marking-stage inclusions, and for the $P$- and $J$-actions. In particular, $$\label{eq:perfect-H-cohomology} H^aC^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(H,R;T)= \begin{cases} C_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,\\ C_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,\ell),&a=3,\\ 0,&a\ne0,3. \end{cases}$$ The two lines are discrete. The residual $P/H$-action on them is trivial, $J$ acts trivially on $k$, and $\mathcal O_F^\times\subset J$ acts on $\ell$ by $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Natural coefficient inclusions compare root-stage cochains with completed-stage cochains, preserving profinite parameters and both stabilizer actions.
- **claim-continuous-extension-descent** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-continuous-extension)): Let $1\to N\to G\to Q\to1$ be an extension of compact profinite groups whose quotient map has a continuous section as a map of spaces. Let $M$ be a complete continuous coefficient module with invariant neighborhoods, or a filtered union of complete $G$-stable stages with the convention of Definition <a href="#def:stage-cochains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:stage-cochains">5</a>.* *If continuous $N$-cohomology satisfies $$H^tC^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(N,M;T)=C_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,V_t)$$ for all compact profinite $T$, naturally in $T$ and for the quotient action, with $V_t$ discrete, there is the natural first-quadrant spectral sequence $$\label{eq:continuous-HS} H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,V_t) \Longrightarrow H^{s+t}C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(G,M).$$ The same construction includes an additional profinite parameter. More generally, a coefficient map inducing quasi-isomorphisms on $N$-cochains with all such parameters induces a quasi-isomorphism on $G$-cochains.
  - Explanation: A continuous space section and the parameterized kernel-cohomology property yield quotient descent and transport coefficient quasi-isomorphisms to the full group.
- **claim-ordinary-coefficient-calculation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-coefficient-calculation)): The natural inclusion $L\hookrightarrow R$ induces, for every compact profinite $T$, a quasi-isomorphism $$\label{eq:ordinary-perfect-P-comparison} C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(P,L;T)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(P,R;T).$$ It is $J$-equivariant and natural in $T$. With discrete cohomology groups, evaluation gives $$\label{eq:ordinary-four-rows} H^aC^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(P,L;T)= \begin{cases} C_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,1,\\ C_{\mathrm{cts}}(T,\ell),&a=3,4,\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$ Here the identifications in degrees one and four use the same nonzero element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{cts}}(1+3\mathbb Z_3,\mathbb F_p)$. The $J$-action is trivial in degrees zero and one. On each upper line, its restriction to $\mathcal O_F^\times$ is the nontrivial norm character $\delta$.
  - Explanation: The ordinary marking inclusion becomes a quasi-isomorphism for the full stabilizer. Its lower cohomology lines are constant and its upper lines carry the norm character.
- **claim-spherical-constants-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-spherical-constants)): There is a finite étale $S$-algebra $S_k$ with $\pi_0S_k=W(k)$. If $d=[k:\mathbb F_p]$, then $$\label{eq:spherical-constants} \pi_*S_k=\pi_*S\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}W(k),\qquad S_k\simeq S^{\oplus d} \quad\text{as $S$-modules}.$$ The coefficient inclusions give canonical maps $S_k\to E_i(k)$, and stabilizer automorphisms fixing $k$ are $S_k$-linear. Set $$\label{eq:test-spectra} Z_k=L_{K(3)}S_k,\qquad Y=L_{K(2)}(E_2\wedge Z_k).$$ The algebra $E_3(k)$ is descendable over $Z_k$ in the $K(3)$-local category. For its Amitsur object $$\label{eq:morava-amitsur} C^s=\underbrace{E_3\otimes^{K(3)}_{Z_k}\cdots \otimes^{K(3)}_{Z_k}E_3}_{s+1\text{ factors}},$$ completed Morava cooperations identify $$\label{eq:unextended-cooperations} \pi_*C^s=\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(P^s,E_{3*}),$$ with the adic coefficient topology and the usual evaluation and stabilizer-action faces.
  - Explanation: Finite spherical constants are finite free as underlying modules and select unextended Morava descent with its actual continuous bar faces.
- **claim-finite-totalization-cubes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-totalizations)): Let $X^\bullet$ be a cosimplicial object in a stable $\infty$-category. For $r\geq0$, let $\mathcal P_0([r])$ be the poset of nonempty subsets of $[r]=\{0,\ldots,r\}$. The functor $$g_r:\mathcal P_0([r])\longrightarrow\Delta_{\leq r},\qquad S\longmapsto[\|S\|-1],$$ sends an inclusion to the injection recording the positions of its ordered elements. Here $\Delta_{\leq r}$ is the full subcategory on $[0],\ldots,[r]$, including all codegeneracies. Restriction induces $$\label{eq:finite-totalization-cube} \mathop{\mathrm{Tot}}_r X^\bullet=\lim_{\Delta_{\leq r}}X^\bullet \simeq\lim_{S\in\mathcal P_0([r])}X^{\|S\|-1}.$$ These equivalences respect the totalization tower and the augmentation when $X^\bullet$ is augmented. Every exact functor between stable $\infty$-categories therefore preserves these partial totalizations as an augmented tower.
  - Explanation: Finite punctured cubes compute full truncated totalizations, including codegeneracies, so exact functors preserve the augmented partial tower.
- **claim-tested-descent-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-descent-test)): The natural augmentation induces an equivalence $$\label{eq:tested-totalization} Y/p\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Tot}}\mathcal T(C^\bullet).$$ The corresponding spectral sequence $$\label{eq:tested-spectral-sequence} \mathsf E_2^{s,t} =H^s\bigl(\pi_t\mathcal T(C^\bullet)\bigr) \Longrightarrow\pi_{t-s}(Y/p)$$ is strongly convergent, with a horizontal vanishing line at a finite page and a finite filtration on its abutment. Its differentials are $k[[x]]$-linear and $J$-semilinear-equivariant.* *There is a compatible diagonal summand, denoted by a subscript $\Delta$, on every page and on the abutment filtration: it is the summand on which the two copies of $k$, from $E_2$ and $S_k$, agree. The same assertions hold on that summand.
  - Explanation: Tested descent converges to actual homotopy with a finite filtration, and its diagonal constant-field summand retains the coefficient actions.
- **claim-ordinary-cooperations-residue** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-cooperations)): The ordinary, unlocalized smash product has even homotopy and $$\label{eq:ordinary-kunneth} \pi_*(E_2\wedge C^s) =(E_2)_*(E_3)\otimes_{E_{3*}} \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(P^s,E_{3*}).$$ This module is flat over $E_{2*}$. In particular, $D_m^s$ is flat over $A_m$, and reduction by $(p,x^m)$ creates no odd homotopy. There are natural identifications $$\label{eq:residue-cochains} D_1^s= \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(P^s,K)\otimes_K L =\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_U\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(P^s,L^U),$$ compatible with the bar maps and the marking actions.
  - Explanation: Ordinary cooperations are even and flat. Their residue algebra is precisely the finite-stage marking cochain algebra, with the actual bar and marking maps.
- **claim-completed-homotopy-terms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-completed-test-terms)): The diagonal homotopy cochains of $\mathcal T(C^\bullet)$ are zero in odd internal degrees. In degree $2j$ they are naturally $$\label{eq:completed-homotopy-cochains} \left(\lim_m D_m^\bullet\right) \otimes_{k[[x]]}\Omega^{\otimes j}.$$ The maps to the finite quotients on homotopy groups are the ordinary reduction maps.
  - Explanation: Completed tested homotopy is the inverse limit of ordinary jet quotients tensored with the actual periodicity line, and odd internal degrees vanish.
- **claim-split-deformation-jet-lifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-split-jet-lift)): There are compatible maps of $k$-algebras $$\label{eq:split-parameter-map} \varphi_m:A\longrightarrow R[x]/(x^m),\qquad m\geq1,$$ reducing to $a\mapsto0$, $t\mapsto t$ at $x=0$, with the following property. The pulled-back $p$-divisible group is isomorphic, with its specified identification at $x=0$, to the direct sum of the universal height-two equal-characteristic deformation modulo $x^m$ and the constant height-one étale group.* *The maps and isomorphisms respect $P$, acting on $R$ and fixing $x$, and $J$, acting both on $R$ by marking changes and on $k[[x]]$ by the height-two deformation action. For fixed $m$, the parameter map is continuous into one complete finite root/marking stage with nilpotent variable $x$. Any finite set of coefficients of the formal-group isomorphism lies in such a stage after a further finite enlargement.
  - Explanation: Compatible coefficient maps lift the marked split deformation through every jet, equivariantly and with finite-stage control for each finite set of coefficients.
- **claim-all-jet-cochain-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-jet-comparison)): For every $m\geq1$ there is a natural, compatible, $J$-equivariant quasi-isomorphism of cochain complexes $$\label{eq:finite-jet-map} D_m^\bullet\longrightarrow C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(P,R)[x]/x^m,$$ where the cochains on $R$ have the finite-stage convention of Definition <a href="#def:stage-cochains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:stage-cochains">5</a>. The action of $P$ on the target fixes $x$, and $J$ acts through its actions on $R$ and the height-two coefficients. Consequently, in each internal degree $2j$, the diagonal homotopy cochains of the spectral sequence in Proposition <a href="#prop:descent-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:descent-test">44</a> are naturally quasi-isomorphic to $$\label{eq:all-jet-comparison} \left(\lim_mC^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(P,R)[x]/x^m\right) \otimes_{k[[x]]}\Omega^{\otimes j}.$$ Odd internal degrees vanish. In particular, $$\label{eq:homotopy-second-page} \mathsf E_{2,\Delta}^{s,2j} =H^s(P,R)[[x]]\otimes_{k[[x]]}\Omega^{\otimes j}.$$ Here the coefficient groups are the actual representations of Theorem <a href="#thm:coefficient-calculation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:coefficient-calculation">41</a>: they are $k$ in degrees $0,1$, the one-dimensional line $\ell$ in degrees $3,4$, and zero otherwise. The action of $J$ on the first two lines is trivial, and $\ell\|_{\mathcal O_F^\times}=k(\delta)$.
  - Explanation: Every cooperation jet compares naturally with perfected coefficient cochains. The inverse limit retains the realized stabilizer action and identifies the tested second page.
- **claim-generic-norm-character-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-generic-character)): The fixed field $Q_x^{G_F}$ is $k$. Moreover, $$Q_x(\delta)\not\simeq\overline\omega^{\otimes b} \qquad\text{for every }b\in\mathbb Z$$ as semilinear $G_F$-representations.
  - Explanation: The full quadratic unit group has only constant invariant Laurent functions, and its norm-character line is never a periodicity power.
- **claim-generic-diagonal-homotopy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-generic-homotopy)): The diagonal spectral sequence of Proposition <a href="#prop:descent-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:descent-test">44</a>, after extension of scalars to $Q_x$, collapses at its second page. For every $r\in\mathbb Z$, its actual abutment filtration gives $G_F$-equivariant short exact sequences $$\begin{aligned} 0&\longrightarrow Q_x(\delta)\otimes_{Q_x}\overline\omega^{\otimes(r+2)} \longrightarrow\mathcal V_{2r}^\Delta(Z_k) \longrightarrow\overline\omega^{\otimes r}\longrightarrow0, \label{eq:generic-even-filtration}\\ 0&\longrightarrow Q_x(\delta)\otimes_{Q_x}\overline\omega^{\otimes(r+1)} \longrightarrow\mathcal V_{2r-1}^\Delta(Z_k) \longrightarrow\overline\omega^{\otimes r}\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:generic-odd-filtration} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, each diagonal summand $\mathcal V_d^\Delta(Z_k)$ has dimension two over $Q_x$, and $\mathcal V_{-3}^\Delta(Z_k)$ contains a subrepresentation isomorphic to $Q_x(\delta)$.
  - Explanation: The localized diagonal spectral sequence collapses. Its actual filtration exhibits a norm-twisted subrepresentation and a periodicity quotient, without asserting an equivariant splitting.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-section-facts",
        "exact_quote": "There are natural isomorphisms of additive sheaves $$\\mathcal H_i\\simeq \\mathcal H^0(V_i),\\qquad\n \\mathcal H^0(\\mathcal O)=\\underline{\\mathbb Q_p}.$$ The automorphism sheaf of $V_i$ is the locally profinite group $D_i^\\times$. A family fiberwise isomorphic to $V_i$ is locally of that form on the perfectoid site. Trivial bundles and these positive basic bundles have no higher relative cohomology sheaves. For such bundles, $\\underline{\\mathbb Q_p}$-linear maps between the section sheaves are exactly the maps of bundles, relatively and compatibly with base change.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-section-facts",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stable-bundle-tests",
        "exact_quote": "A section of $V_3$ nonzero on every geometric fiber gives a subbundle $\\mathcal O\\hookrightarrow V_3$ with quotient locally isomorphic to $V_2$. Conversely, an extension $$0\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O\\longrightarrow E\n       \\longrightarrow V_2\\longrightarrow0\n \\label{eq:basic-extension}$$ has middle bundle fiberwise $V_3$ if and only if its extension class is nonzero on every geometric fiber. Moreover, $\\mathcal H^0(V_2^\\vee)=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stable-bundle-tests",

Moreover, $\\mathcal H^0(V_2^\\vee)=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-integral-frames",
        "exact_quote": "There exists a determinant identification $\\iota:\\det V_3\\xrightarrow{\\sim}\\det V_2$ over $k$ for which the following conclusions hold. Fix such an identification for the sequel. There is an isomorphism $$\\widetilde X\\simeq\\operatorname{Surj}(V_3,V_2).\n \\label{eq:connected-surjections}$$ A surjection has a unique kernel frame for which its determinant identification is $\\iota$. Under <a href=\"#eq:connected-surjections\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:connected-surjections\">[eq:connected-surjections]</a>, this is an integral étale Tate generator for the corresponding deformation. The source-frame group $P$ acts by reduced norm on this normalization, and a target-frame change in $J$ has its corresponding constant determinant adjustment.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-perfected-marking-torsor",
        "exact_quote": "For each open $U\\subset J$, the map $$\\widetilde X\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spa}}(B^\\flat),\\qquad B=L^U,$$ is a pro-étale $U$-torsor.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-two-towers",
        "exact_quote": "For each open $U\\subset J$ and $B=L^U$ there is a natural equivalence of $v$-stacks over $k$ $$\\simeq[N^*/U].\n \\label{eq:two-towers}$$ It is obtained from the commuting torsor presentations $$\\widetilde X/U\\simeq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spa}}(B^\\flat),\\qquad\n \\widetilde X/H\\simeq N^*.$$ The residual group $P/H=\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$ acts on $N^*$ by kernel scalars through reduced norm, up to the common inverse convention. The $J$-action changes the quotient frame and makes the kernel determinant adjustment in <a href=\"#eq:normalized-frame-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:normalized-frame-action\">[eq:normalized-frame-action]</a>. All these identifications commute with base change and products with profinite parameter spaces.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-two-towers",

The $J$-action changes the quotient frame and makes the kernel determinant adjustment in <a href=\"#eq:normalized-frame-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:normalized-frame-action\">[eq:normalized-frame-action]</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-etale-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L=\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_i B_i$ be an injective union of finite étale $K$-algebras, with their maximum valuation norms, and let $C=\\widehat{L^{\\mathrm{perf}}}$. For every finite étale $L$-algebra $E$, the map $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{L\\text{-alg}}(E,L)\\longrightarrow\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{L\\text{-alg}}(E,C)$$ is bijective.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lift-torsors",
        "exact_quote": "The scheme of lifts in $G_L[p^l]$ of the normalized étale generator has coordinate algebra $$R_l=L^{1/p^{2l}}=L^{1/d^l}\\subset R=L^{\\mathrm{perf}}.\n \\label{eq:lift-rings}$$ It is a torsor under the marked group $\\Gamma_2[p^l]_L$. The transition maps give the indicated inclusions inside $R$. The action of $H$ commutes with translation by this constant group. The other frame actions conjugate translations by continuous constant Honda automorphisms and scalar norm units.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-negative-presentation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C^\\flat$ be an algebraically closed perfectoid field of characteristic $p$ containing $k$, choose an algebraically closed untilt $C/\\mathbb C_p$, and let $F/\\mathbb Q_p$ be unramified quadratic. After choosing its embedding at the untilt divisor there are isomorphisms $$N_{C^\\flat}\\simeq\n       (\\mathbb G_{a,C})^\\diamond/\\underline F,\n \\qquad\n (N^*)_{C^\\flat}\\simeq\n       [ (\\mathbb A^1_C\\setminus\\underline F)^\\diamond/\n          \\underline F].\n \\label{eq:negative-presentation}$$ Here $\\mathbb A^1_C$ is the analytic affine line. In the second expression the $F$-support is removed fiberwise before taking the translation quotient. The action of $F^\\times$ through the quotient frame is multiplication in this presentation, up to inversion and the choice of unramified embedding. The additional kernel determinant adjustment from Theorem <a href=\"#thm:two-towers\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:two-towers\">13</a> is a scalar in $\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-negative-presentation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C^\\flat$ be an algebraically closed perfectoid field of characteristic $p$ containing $k$, choose an algebraically closed untilt $C/\\mathbb C_p$, and let $F/\\mathbb Q_p$ be unramified quadratic. After choosing its embedding at the untilt divisor there are isomorphisms $$N_{C^\\flat}\\simeq\n       (\\mathbb G_{a,C})^\\diamond/\\underline F,\n \\qquad\n (N^*)_{C^\\flat}\\simeq\n       [ (\\mathbb A^1_C\\setminus\\underline F)^\\diamond/\n          \\underline F].\n \\label{eq:negative-presentation}$$ Here $\\mathbb A^1_C$ is the analytic affine line. In the second expression the $F$-support is removed fiberwise before taking the translation quotient. The action of $F^\\times$ through the quotient frame is multiplication in this presentation, up to inversion and the choice of unramified embedding. The additional kernel determinant adjustment from Theorem <a href=\"#thm:two-towers\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:two-towers\">13</a> is a scalar in $\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $C^\\flat$ be an algebraically closed perfectoid field of characteristic $p$ containing $k$, choose an algebraically closed untilt $C/\\mathbb C_p$, and let $F/\\mathbb Q_p$ be unramified quadratic.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-resolution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a compact $p$-adic analytic group with no element of order $p$, and put $d=\\dim G$. The trivial module $\\mathbb F_p$ admits a length-$d$ resolution $Q_\\bullet$ by finitely generated projective $\\mathbb F_p[[G]]$-modules, with their compact topologies. If $M$ is a complete linearly topologized $\\mathbb F_p$-representation with invariant neighborhoods, then $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathbb F_p[[G]],\\mathrm{cts}}(Q_\\bullet,M)\n \\simeq C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,M)$$ by continuous chain-homotopy equivalences. Each term on the left is a continuous direct summand of a finite power of $M$.*\n\n*For $G=H$ the dimension is eight, and the reversed group-ring dual of $Q_\\bullet$ resolves the trivial right $\\mathbb F_p[[H]]$-module. In particular, continuous $H$-cohomology of these coefficients vanishes above degree eight, and finite coefficient modules have finite cohomology.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Each term on the left is a continuous direct summand of a finite power of $M$.*\n\n*For $G=H$ the dimension is eight, and the reversed group-ring dual of $Q_\\bullet$ resolves the trivial right $\\mathbb F_p[[H]]$-module.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-analytic-roos",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$M_0 \\xleftarrow{f_0} M_1 \\xleftarrow{f_1} M_2\n       \\xleftarrow{} \\cdots$$ be a countable inverse system of complete nonarchimedean normed abelian groups. Suppose every $f_n$ is a contraction with dense image. Then the map $$\\prod_{n\\geq0}M_n\\longrightarrow\\prod_{n\\geq0}M_n,\\qquad\n (x_n)\\longmapsto(x_n-f_n(x_{n+1}))\n \\label{eq:analytic-roos}$$ is surjective. The same assertion holds after replacing every $M_n$ by $\\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,M_n)$. Consequently these systems have no positive derived inverse limits.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },

The same assertion holds after replacing every $M_n$ by $\\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,M_n)$. Consequently these systems have no positive derived inverse limits.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-geometric-cohomology",
        "exact_quote": "For every compact profinite $T$, the geometric period domain has cohomology $$H^i_v(T\\times(N^\\ast)_{C^\\flat},\\mathcal O^\\flat)\n =\n \\begin{cases}\n R_T,&i=0,\\\\\n R_T\\otimes_{C^\\flat}\\ell_C,&i=3,\\\\\n 0,&i\\neq0,3,\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:analytic-geometric-rows}$$ where $\\ell_C$ is a one-dimensional $C^\\flat$-space. All identifications are natural under continuous maps of $T$ and the frame-group actions. On $\\ell_C$, $\\mathcal O_F^\\times\\subset J$ acts through the nontrivial character $$\\delta(u)=N_{F/\\mathbb Q_p}(u)\\bmod3.$$ The kernel-scalar action of $\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$ is trivial. The line spaces in <a href=\"#eq:analytic-geometric-rows\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:analytic-geometric-rows\">[eq:analytic-geometric-rows]</a> have their usual topology, as expressed by the continuous-parameter formula.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-geometric-cohomology",
          "exact_quote": "For every compact profinite $T$, the geometric period domain has cohomology $$H^i_v(T\\times(N^\\ast)_{C^\\flat},\\mathcal O^\\flat)\n =\n \\begin{cases}\n R_T,&i=0,\\\\\n R_T\\otimes_{C^\\flat}\\ell_C,&i=3,\\\\\n 0,&i\\neq0,3,\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:analytic-geometric-rows}$$ where $\\ell_C$ is a one-dimensional $C^\\flat$-space. All identifications are natural under continuous maps of $T$ and the frame-group actions. On $\\ell_C$, $\\mathcal O_F^\\times\\subset J$ acts through the nontrivial character $$\\delta(u)=N_{F/\\mathbb Q_p}(u)\\bmod3.$$ The kernel-scalar action of $\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$ is trivial. The line spaces in <a href=\"#eq:analytic-geometric-rows\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:analytic-geometric-rows\">[eq:analytic-geometric-rows]</a> have their usual topology, as expressed by the continuous-parameter formula.",

        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every compact profinite $T$, the geometric period domain has cohomology $$H^i_v(T\\times(N^\\ast)_{C^\\flat},\\mathcal O^\\flat)\n =\n \\begin{cases}\n R_T,&i=0,\\\\\n R_T\\otimes_{C^\\flat}\\ell_C,&i=3,\\\\\n 0,&i\\neq0,3,\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:analytic-geometric-rows}$$ where $\\ell_C$ is a one-dimensional $C^\\flat$-space. All identifications are natural under continuous maps of $T$ and the frame-group actions. On $\\ell_C$, $\\mathcal O_F^\\times\\subset J$ acts through the nontrivial character $$\\delta(u)=N_{F/\\mathbb Q_p}(u)\\bmod3.$$ The kernel-scalar action of $\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$ is trivial. The line spaces in <a href=\"#eq:analytic-geometric-rows\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:analytic-geometric-rows\">[eq:analytic-geometric-rows]</a> have their usual topology, as expressed by the continuous-parameter formula.",
      "explanation": "The geometric period domain has the constant line and an upper orientation line carrying the nontrivial residue norm character; kernel scalars act trivially.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-frobenius-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a finite product of finite separable extensions of $k((t))$. For every compact profinite $Q$ there is an exact sequence $$0\\longrightarrow\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(Q,B^\\flat)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(Q,\\mathcal A_B)\n \\xrightarrow{\\Phi-1}\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(Q,\\mathcal A_B)\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:analytic-frobenius-exact}$$ It is natural in $B,Q$ and continuous actions over $k$. In particular, for a compact group $G$ acting continuously on $B$, over $k$ and preserving its valuations, the natural map identifies actual cochain complexes by $$C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,B^\\flat;T)\n \\simeq\n \\operatorname{fib}\\left(\n \\Phi-1:C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,\\mathcal A_B;T)\n               \\longrightarrow C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,\\mathcal A_B;T)\\right).\n \\label{eq:analytic-frobenius-cochains}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-completed-calculation",
        "exact_quote": "For a cofinal family of sufficiently small open normal subgroups $U\\subset J$, put $B=L^U$. Then $H^a_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\\flat)$ is finite for every $a$, and it is zero for $a>7$. For every compact profinite $T$, evaluation induces the natural identification $$H^aC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\\flat;T)\n =\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}\n       \\bigl(T,H^a_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\\flat)\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:analytic-finite-stage-parameters}$$ where the finite group on the right is discrete. Moreover, $$(B^\\flat)^H=k,\\qquad L^H=k,\n \\label{eq:analytic-invariant-field}$$ and $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_U H^aC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^\\flat;T)\n =\n \\begin{cases}\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,\\\\\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,\\ell),&a=3,\\\\\n 0,&a\\neq0,3.\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:analytic-completed-colimit}$$ The colimit identifications respect restrictions, conjugations and the $J$-action. The residual $P/H=\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$-action on both lines is trivial; on the upper line $\\mathcal O_F^\\times\\subset J$ acts by $\\delta$. The statements are natural in $T$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-duality",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a compact continuous $\\mathbb F_p$-representation of $H$ and give $$M^\\vee=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathbb F_p,\\mathrm{cts}}(M,\\mathbb F_p)$$ the discrete topology and the contragredient action. Continuous $H$-cohomology of $M$ is compact Hausdorff in its ordinary cochain quotient topology, and there are natural isomorphisms $$\\label{eq:compact-cohomology-duality}\n H^a_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,M)^\\vee\n \\cong H^{8-a}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,M^\\vee).$$ The transposed coefficient map induces the dual cohomology map.         "source_id": "PM-318.4"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-strictness",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A^\\bullet$ be a complex of complete, separable, metrizable, linearly topologized $\\mathbb F_p$-spaces with continuous linear differentials. If $H^a(A^\\bullet)$ is finite, the boundary subgroup is open and closed in the cycle group, and the differential onto that boundary subgroup is open. Thus, for every neighborhood $V$ of zero in $A^{a-1}$, sufficiently small $a$-cocycles have primitives in $V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-strictness",

Thus, for every neighborhood $V$ of zero in $A^{a-1}$, sufficiently small $a$-cocycles have primitives in $V$.",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-profinite-parameters",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be an arbitrary compact profinite space.*\n\n1.  *A continuous map from $T$ to $F$ lifts across an open continuous surjection $E\\twoheadrightarrow F$ of complete metrizable linearly topologized groups.*\n\n\n2.  *If a complex $A^\\bullet$ of complete, separable, metrizable, linearly topologized $\\mathbb F_p$-spaces with continuous linear differentials has finite cohomology, then naturally $$\\label{eq:finite-cohomology-parameters}\n     H^a C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,A^\\bullet)\n     \\cong C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,H^a(A^\\bullet)),$$ where the group on the right is discrete.*\n\n3.  *For a filtered diagram of such complexes, this identity commutes with the cochain colimit, with the colimit of cohomology groups given the discrete topology.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-profinite-parameters",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be an arbitrary compact profinite space.*\n\n1.  *A continuous map from $T$ to $F$ lifts across an open continuous surjection $E\\twoheadrightarrow F$ of complete metrizable linearly topologized groups.*\n\n\n2.  *If a complex $A^\\bullet$ of complete, separable, metrizable, linearly topologized $\\mathbb F_p$-spaces with continuous linear differentials has finite cohomology, then naturally $$\\label{eq:finite-cohomology-parameters}\n     H^a C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,A^\\bullet)\n     \\cong C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,H^a(A^\\bullet)),$$ where the group on the right is discrete.*\n\n3.  *For a filtered diagram of such complexes, this identity commutes with the cochain colimit, with the colimit of cohomology groups given the discrete topology.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $T$ be an arbitrary compact profinite space.*\n\n1.  *A continuous map from $T$ to $F$ lifts across an open continuous surjection $E\\twoheadrightarrow F$ of complete metrizable linearly topologized groups.*\n\n\n2.  *If a complex $A^\\bullet$ of complete, separable, metrizable, linearly topologized $\\mathbb F_p$-spaces with continuous linear differentials has finite cohomology, then naturally $$\\label{eq:finite-cohomology-parameters}\n     H^a C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,A^\\bullet)\n     \\cong C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,H^a(A^\\bullet)),$$ where the group on the right is discrete.*\n\n3.  *For a filtered diagram of such complexes, this identity commutes with the cochain colimit, with the colimit of cohomology groups given the discrete topology.",
      "explanation": "Open surjections lift continuous profinite families; finite cohomology therefore commutes with continuous parameters and the prescribed filtered cochain colimits.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-distribution-operator",
        "exact_quote": "After the fixed finite enlargement of $k$, the inverse-limit distribution algebra of the translation tower is $k[[D]]$. A parameter $D$ can be chosen with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *Its action on $R$ is $L$-linear and locally nilpotent, commutes with $H$, and satisfies $$\\label{eq:distribution-kernels}\n     \\ker(D^{p^i}:R\\to R)=L^{1/p^i}\\quad(i\\ge0),\n     \\qquad D:R\\twoheadrightarrow R.$$*\n\n2.  *The order-two norm quotient acts on $D$ by its nontrivial character. The action of $J$ on $k[[D]]$ is continuous on finite jets.*\n\n\n3.  *If $i$ is positive, even, and divisible by $[k:\\mathbb F_p]$, and $q=p^i$, then $$\\label{eq:distribution-frobenius}\n     D(f^q)=(D^qf)^q,\\qquad\n     D^q(af)=aD^qf\\quad(a\\in L^{1/q}).$$*\n\n4.  *Each operator on a fixed root stage is continuous after passage to a finite larger marking stage. Every finite target stage has a continuous additive lift under $D$ at some finite larger marking and root stage. Thus the sequence with kernel $L$ and operator $D$ is exact on the prescribed cochains, also with profinite parameters.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-distribution-operator",

The action of $J$ on $k[[D]]$ is continuous on finite jets.*\n\n\n3.  *If $i$ is positive, even, and divisible by $[k:\\mathbb F_p]$, and $q=p^i$, then $$\\label{eq:distribution-frobenius}\n     D(f^q)=(D^qf)^q,\\qquad\n     D^q(af)=aD^qf\\quad(a\\in L^{1/q}).$$*\n\n4.  *Each operator on a fixed root stage is continuous after passage to a finite larger marking stage. Every finite target stage has a continuous additive lift under $D$ at some finite larger marking and root stage. Thus the sequence with kernel $L$ and operator $D$ is exact on the prescribed cochains, also with profinite parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cartier-traces",
        "exact_quote": "For every $i\\ge1$, with $q=p^i$, there is $c_i\\in k^\\times$ such that $$\\label{eq:Cartier-distribution-trace}\n D^{q-1}(f^{1/q})=c_i C_0^i(f)\\qquad(f\\in L).$$ In particular, $C_0(f^p)=0$. If $B=L^U$ contains $\\eta$ as a basis, then $$\\label{eq:Cartier-exact-sequence}\n 0\\longrightarrow B\\xrightarrow{\\mathrm{Fr}}B\n \\xrightarrow{d/\\eta}B\\xrightarrow{C_0}B\\longrightarrow0$$ is exact and its two short exact factors have continuous additive sections onto their images.*\n\n\n\n*Write $B=\\prod_j\\kappa_j((s_j))$ and $\\eta=h_j(s_j)\\,ds_j/s_j$ on its factors. Define $$M=\\{f:\\operatorname{ord}_{s_j}(f_jh_j)>0\\text{ for all }j\\},\n \\qquad\n M^0=\\{f:\\operatorname{ord}_{s_j}(f_jh_j)\\ge0\\text{ for all }j\\}.$$ These are compact open $H$- and $C_0$-stable lattices, and $M^0/M$ is finite. For the pairing $$\\label{eq:residue-pairing}\n \\langle f,g\\rangle_B\n =\\sum_j\\operatorname{Tr}_{\\kappa_j/\\mathbb F_p}\n             \\operatorname{Res}_{s_j}(f_jg_j\\eta)$$ there are topological $H$-equivariant identifications $$\\label{eq:residue-lattice-duals}\n M^\\vee=B/\\mathcal O_B,\\qquad\n (B/M)^\\vee=\\mathcal O_B,\\qquad\n \\langle C_0f,g\\rangle_B=\\langle f,g^p\\rangle_B.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-good-stages",
        "exact_quote": "There is a cofinal collection of open normal uniform subgroups $U\\subset J$ for which $B=L^U$ has all the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\eta$ is a basis over $B$ and $H^a_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\\flat)$ is finite for every $a$;*\n\n2.  *there is $\\theta_0\\in B^{1/p}$ with $D\\theta_0=1$;*\n\n\n3.  *for every $\\sigma\\in U$, $$\\label{eq:good-stage-jets}\n     \\sigma(D)-D\\in D^{p+2}k[[D]].$$*\n\n*For these stages $D$ restricts to a continuous $H$-equivariant map $B^{1/p}\\to B^{1/p}$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-operator",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be a compact Hausdorff $\\mathbb F_p$-vector space, let $C:N\\to N$ be continuous and $\\mathbb F_p$-linear, and suppose that $$I=\\bigcap_{n\\ge0}C^nN\n \\quad\\text{and}\\quad N/CN$$ are finite. Then $\\ker C$ is finite, and every subgroup of $N$ whose elements have finite forward $C$-orbits is finite. If $f:N\\to X$ is a homomorphism with finite kernel, then $$\\label{eq:stable-image-finite-fiber}\n \\bigcap_{n\\ge0}f(C^nN)=f(I).$$ No topology on $X$ is required in this last assertion.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-operator",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be a compact Hausdorff $\\mathbb F_p$-vector space, let $C:N\\to N$ be continuous and $\\mathbb F_p$-linear, and suppose that $$I=\\bigcap_{n\\ge0}C^nN\n \\quad\\text{and}\\quad N/CN$$ are finite. Then $\\ker C$ is finite, and every subgroup of $N$ whose elements have finite forward $C$-orbits is finite. If $f:N\\to X$ is a homomorphism with finite kernel, then $$\\label{eq:stable-image-finite-fiber}\n \\bigcap_{n\\ge0}f(C^nN)=f(I).$$ No topology on $X$ is required in this last assertion.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $N$ be a compact Hausdorff $\\mathbb F_p$-vector space, let $C:N\\to N$ be continuous and $\\mathbb F_p$-linear, and suppose that $$I=\\bigcap_{n\\ge0}C^nN\n \\quad\\text{and}\\quad N/CN$$ are finite. Then $\\ker C$ is finite, and every subgroup of $N$ whose elements have finite forward $C$-orbits is finite.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-decompletion",
        "exact_quote": "For every compact profinite space $T$, the natural coefficient inclusions induce a quasi-isomorphism $$\\label{eq:natural-decompletion}\n C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,R;T)\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_{U,e}C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^{1/p^e};T)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_UC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^\\flat;T).$$ It is natural in $T$, in marking-stage inclusions, and for the $P$- and $J$-actions. In particular, $$\\label{eq:perfect-H-cohomology}\n H^aC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,R;T)=\n \\begin{cases}\n C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,\\\\\n C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,\\ell),&a=3,\\\\\n 0,&a\\ne0,3.\n \\end{cases}$$ The two lines are discrete. The residual $P/H$-action on them is trivial, $J$ acts trivially on $k$, and $\\mathcal O_F^\\times\\subset J$ acts on $\\ell$ by $\\delta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-decompletion",
          "exact_quote": "For every compact profinite space $T$, the natural coefficient inclusions induce a quasi-isomorphism $$\\label{eq:natural-decompletion}\n C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,R;T)\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_{U,e}C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^{1/p^e};T)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_UC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^\\flat;T).$$ It is natural in $T$, in marking-stage inclusions, and for the $P$- and $J$-actions. In particular, $$\\label{eq:perfect-H-cohomology}\n H^aC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,R;T)=\n \\begin{cases}\n C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,\\\\\n C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,\\ell),&a=3,\\\\\n 0,&a\\ne0,3.\n \\end{cases}$$ The two lines are discrete. The residual $P/H$-action on them is trivial, $J$ acts trivially on $k$, and $\\mathcal O_F^\\times\\subset J$ acts on $\\ell$ by $\\delta$.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every compact profinite space $T$, the natural coefficient inclusions induce a quasi-isomorphism $$\\label{eq:natural-decompletion}\n C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,R;T)\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_{U,e}C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^{1/p^e};T)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_UC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^\\flat;T).$$ It is natural in $T$, in marking-stage inclusions, and for the $P$- and $J$-actions. In particular, $$\\label{eq:perfect-H-cohomology}\n H^aC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,R;T)=\n \\begin{cases}\n C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,\\\\\n C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,\\ell),&a=3,\\\\\n 0,&a\\ne0,3.\n \\end{cases}$$ The two lines are discrete.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-continuous-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $1\\to N\\to G\\to Q\\to1$ be an extension of compact profinite groups whose quotient map has a continuous section as a map of spaces. Let $M$ be a complete continuous coefficient module with invariant neighborhoods, or a filtered union of complete $G$-stable stages with the convention of Definition <a href=\"#def:stage-cochains\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:stage-cochains\">5</a>.*\n\n\n*If continuous $N$-cohomology satisfies $$H^tC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(N,M;T)=C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,V_t)$$ for all compact profinite $T$, naturally in $T$ and for the quotient action, with $V_t$ discrete, there is the natural first-quadrant spectral sequence $$\\label{eq:continuous-HS}\n H^s_{\\mathrm{cts}}(Q,V_t)\n \\Longrightarrow H^{s+t}C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,M).$$ The same construction includes an additional profinite parameter. More generally, a coefficient map inducing quasi-isomorphisms on $N$-cochains with all such parameters induces a quasi-isomorphism on $G$-cochains.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-continuous-extension",
          "exact_quote": "Let $1\\to N\\to G\\to Q\\to1$ be an extension of compact profinite groups whose quotient map has a continuous section as a map of spaces. More generally, a coefficient map inducing quasi-isomorphisms on $N$-cochains with all such parameters induces a quasi-isomorphism on $G$-cochains.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-continuous-extension",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a complete continuous coefficient module with invariant neighborhoods, or a filtered union of complete $G$-stable stages with the convention of Definition <a href=\"#def:stage-cochains\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:stage-cochains\">5</a>.*\n\n\n*If continuous $N$-cohomology satisfies $$H^tC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(N,M;T)=C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,V_t)$$ for all compact profinite $T$, naturally in $T$ and for the quotient action, with $V_t$ discrete, there is the natural first-quadrant spectral sequence $$\\label{eq:continuous-HS}\n H^s_{\\mathrm{cts}}(Q,V_t)\n \\Longrightarrow H^{s+t}C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,M).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-coefficient-calculation",
        "exact_quote": "The natural inclusion $L\\hookrightarrow R$ induces, for every compact profinite $T$, a quasi-isomorphism $$\\label{eq:ordinary-perfect-P-comparison}\n C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(P,L;T)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(P,R;T).$$ It is $J$-equivariant and natural in $T$. With discrete cohomology groups, evaluation gives $$\\label{eq:ordinary-four-rows}\n H^aC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(P,L;T)=\n \\begin{cases}\n C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,1,\\\\\n C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,\\ell),&a=3,4,\\\\\n 0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$ Here the identifications in degrees one and four use the same nonzero element of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(1+3\\mathbb Z_3,\\mathbb F_p)$. The $J$-action is trivial in degrees zero and one. On each upper line, its restriction to $\\mathcal O_F^\\times$ is the nontrivial norm character $\\delta$.",

      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-spherical-constants",
        "exact_quote": "There is a finite étale $S$-algebra $S_k$ with $\\pi_0S_k=W(k)$. If $d=[k:\\mathbb F_p]$, then $$\\label{eq:spherical-constants}\n  \\pi_*S_k=\\pi_*S\\otimes_{\\mathbb Z_p}W(k),\\qquad\n  S_k\\simeq S^{\\oplus d}\n  \\quad\\text{as $S$-modules}.$$ The coefficient inclusions give canonical maps $S_k\\to E_i(k)$, and stabilizer automorphisms fixing $k$ are $S_k$-linear. Set $$\\label{eq:test-spectra}\n  Z_k=L_{K(3)}S_k,\\qquad\n  Y=L_{K(2)}(E_2\\wedge Z_k).$$ The algebra $E_3(k)$ is descendable over $Z_k$ in the $K(3)$-local category. For its Amitsur object $$\\label{eq:morava-amitsur}\n  C^s=\\underbrace{E_3\\otimes^{K(3)}_{Z_k}\\cdots\n                    \\otimes^{K(3)}_{Z_k}E_3}_{s+1\\text{ factors}},$$ completed Morava cooperations identify $$\\label{eq:unextended-cooperations}\n  \\pi_*C^s=\\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(P^s,E_{3*}),$$ with the adic coefficient topology and the usual evaluation and stabilizer-action faces.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-totalizations",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X^\\bullet$ be a cosimplicial object in a stable $\\infty$-category. For $r\\geq0$, let $\\mathcal P_0([r])$ be the poset of nonempty subsets of $[r]=\\{0,\\ldots,r\\}$. The functor $$g_r:\\mathcal P_0([r])\\longrightarrow\\Delta_{\\leq r},\\qquad\n S\\longmapsto[|S|-1],$$ sends an inclusion to the injection recording the positions of its ordered elements. Here $\\Delta_{\\leq r}$ is the full subcategory on $[0],\\ldots,[r]$, including all codegeneracies. Restriction induces $$\\label{eq:finite-totalization-cube}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tot}}_r X^\\bullet=\\lim_{\\Delta_{\\leq r}}X^\\bullet\n \\simeq\\lim_{S\\in\\mathcal P_0([r])}X^{|S|-1}.$$ These equivalences respect the totalization tower and the augmentation when $X^\\bullet$ is augmented. Every exact functor between stable $\\infty$-categories therefore preserves these partial totalizations as an augmented tower.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-totalizations",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-descent-test",
        "exact_quote": "The natural augmentation induces an equivalence $$\\label{eq:tested-totalization}\n  Y/p\\simeq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tot}}\\mathcal T(C^\\bullet).$$ The corresponding spectral sequence $$\\label{eq:tested-spectral-sequence}\n  \\mathsf E_2^{s,t}\n     =H^s\\bigl(\\pi_t\\mathcal T(C^\\bullet)\\bigr)\n       \\Longrightarrow\\pi_{t-s}(Y/p)$$ is strongly convergent, with a horizontal vanishing line at a finite page and a finite filtration on its abutment. Its differentials are $k[[x]]$-linear and $J$-semilinear-equivariant.*\n\n*There is a compatible diagonal summand, denoted by a subscript $\\Delta$, on every page and on the abutment filtration: it is the summand on which the two copies of $k$, from $E_2$ and $S_k$, agree.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-cooperations",
        "exact_quote": "The ordinary, unlocalized smash product has even homotopy and $$\\label{eq:ordinary-kunneth}\n  \\pi_*(E_2\\wedge C^s)\n    =(E_2)_*(E_3)\\otimes_{E_{3*}}\n          \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(P^s,E_{3*}).$$ This module is flat over $E_{2*}$. In particular, $D_m^s$ is flat over $A_m$, and reduction by $(p,x^m)$ creates no odd homotopy. There are natural identifications $$\\label{eq:residue-cochains}\n  D_1^s=\n  \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(P^s,K)\\otimes_K L\n    =\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_U\\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(P^s,L^U),$$ compatible with the bar maps and the marking actions.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-split-jet-lift",
        "exact_quote": "There are compatible maps of $k$-algebras $$\\label{eq:split-parameter-map}\n  \\varphi_m:A\\longrightarrow R[x]/(x^m),\\qquad m\\geq1,$$ reducing to $a\\mapsto0$, $t\\mapsto t$ at $x=0$, with the following property. The pulled-back $p$-divisible group is isomorphic, with its specified identification at $x=0$, to the direct sum of the universal height-two equal-characteristic deformation modulo $x^m$ and the constant height-one étale group.*\n\n*The maps and isomorphisms respect $P$, acting on $R$ and fixing $x$, and $J$, acting both on $R$ by marking changes and on $k[[x]]$ by the height-two deformation action. For fixed $m$, the parameter map is continuous into one complete finite root/marking stage with nilpotent variable $x$. Any finite set of coefficients of the formal-group isomorphism lies in such a stage after a further finite enlargement.",
        "source_id": "PM-318.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "There are compatible maps of $k$-algebras $$\\label{eq:split-parameter-map}\n  \\varphi_m:A\\longrightarrow R[x]/(x^m),\\qquad m\\geq1,$$ reducing to $a\\mapsto0$, $t\\mapsto t$ at $x=0$, with the following property.
