<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **ordinary thick subcategory** ([source](#eq:finite-assembly-question)): For objects $X_1,\ldots,X_r$ in the category of $E(n-1)$-local $S$-modules, let $\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}\{X_1,\ldots,X_r\}$ be the smallest full subcategory containing them and closed under equivalences, finite sums, integer suspensions and desuspensions, homotopy cofibers, and retracts (Hovey and Strickland 1999, Definitions 1.2, 1.3(c), and 1.5(c)) [[HS99](#ref-HS99)]
- **generic test and semilinear character line** ([source](#eq:generic-test)): For a spectrum $X$, define $$\label{eq:generic-test} \mathcal T(X)=L_{K(2)}(E_2\wedge X)/p, \qquad \mathcal V_d(X)=\pi_d\mathcal T(X)\otimes_{\mathcal O_x}Q_x.$$
- **connected marking algebra** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Let $L$ be the algebra classifying isomorphisms between the connected formal law of $\mathcal G_K$ and the constant Honda formal group $\Gamma_2$.
- **completed perfection of a finite stage** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For a finite stage $B=L^U$, put $$B_e=B^{1/p^e},\qquad B^{\mathrm{perf}}=\bigcup_{e\geq0}B_e,\qquad B^\flat=\widehat{B^{\mathrm{perf}}}.$$
- **stage cochains** ([source](#stmt-def-stage-cochains)): For $Q=P,H,H'$, or a closed subgroup of one of these groups, cochains with the incomplete coefficients $L$ and $R$ mean $$\begin{aligned} C^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,L;T) &:=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_U \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(T\times Q^s,L^U), \nonumber\\ C^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,R;T) &:=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{U,e} \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}} (T\times Q^s,(L^U)^{1/p^e}). \label{eq:stage-cochains} \end{aligned}$$ We also use the completed-stage complex $$\label{eq:completed-stage-cochains} \mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_U C^\bullet_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,(L^U)^\flat;T).$$ All filtered colimits in these definitions are taken degreewise. Omitting $T$ means that $T$ is a point.
- **locally finite distributions** ([source](#stmt-def-locally-finite-distributions)): For a complete characteristic-$p$ coefficient group $R_0$, define the locally finite distribution module on $F$ by $$\mathcal D_{\mathrm{lf}}(F,R_0) = \varprojlim_n \prod_{\alpha\in F/p^n\mathcal O_F} R_0, \label{eq:analytic-distributions}$$ where the transition sums over the finitely many children of each coset. Equivalently, take the product over $F/\mathcal O_F$ of the distribution modules on those compact open cosets. Every finite-partition module and every product in <a href="#eq:analytic-distributions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:analytic-distributions">[eq:analytic-distributions]</a> carries its product topology.
- **normalized Cartier operator** ([source](#pm-h-33)): Let $\operatorname{Car}$ be intrinsic Cartier on differentials (Cartier 1958, chap. II, Section 6) [[Cartier58](#ref-Cartier58)], and set $$\label{eq:normalized-Cartier} C_0(f)=\frac{\operatorname{Car}(f\eta)}{\eta}.$$
- **ordinary valuation lattices** ([source](#pm-h-34)): For any of our valued algebras $E$, write $E^{++}=\{f:v(f)>0\}$, where positivity is required on every field factor, and write $\mathcal O_E$ for the elements of nonnegative valuation.
- **jet cooperation algebra** ([source](#pm-h-40)): Let $$\label{eq:jet-cooperation-definition} D_m^s= \left(\pi_0(E_2\wedge C^s)/(p,x^m)\right)_\Delta.$$

Every finite-partition module and every product in <a href=\"#eq:analytic-distributions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:analytic-distributions\">[eq:analytic-distributions]</a> carries its product topology.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-mod-p-nullity** [claim](#stmt-lem-mod-p-nullity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mod-p-nullity): Inject module endomorphisms into reduced degree-zero coefficients and apply exact localization. -> The tested homotopy groups are modules over the reduced deformation ring.
- **step-constituent-detector** [claim](#stmt-prop-constituent-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-constituent-test): Use a Serre subcategory and exact triangles to propagate the unit's constituent condition through ordinary finite closure. -> Finite assembly forces periodicity-power constituents.
- **step-finite-p-basis** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-p-basis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-p-basis): Expand over prime-power monomials and preserve the basis under separable base change. -> Ordinary differentials and the marking differential basis.
- **step-primitive-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-lem-primitive-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-primitive-coordinates): Use reduced primitive closures, preparation, and the Frobenius-divided multiplication series. -> Primitive coordinate rings and full generator-lift algebras.
- **step-cover-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-lem-cover-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-coordinates): Take normalized limits using contraction estimates and verify inverse identities and frame compatibility. -> The universal-cover coordinate isomorphism.
- **step-generator-ball** [claim](#stmt-prop-generator-ball) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-generator-ball): Identify complete algebras isometrically on smaller discs and descend the resulting relative isomorphism. -> The entire integral generator tower is the punctured cover.
- **step-relative-sections** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-section-facts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-section-facts): Apply relative section calculations, basic-family classification, and relative full faithfulness. -> Section-sheaf identification and recovery of bundle maps.
- **step-stable-bundle-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-stable-bundle-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stable-bundle-tests): Use geometric slope stability and bundle classification, then descend relative subbundle statements. -> Nonzero sections and nonsplit extensions have the required basic types.
- **step-integral-frames** [claim](#stmt-prop-integral-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-integral-frames): Construct quotient frames, normalize determinants, and reconstruct integral markings from arbitrary surjections. -> Connected markings are determinant-normalized bundle surjections.
- **step-perfected-torsor** [claim](#stmt-lem-perfected-marking-torsor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-perfected-marking-torsor): Pass finite étale torsor identities through perfection, completion, and the affinoid inverse limit. -> Open marking subgroups act by pro-étale torsors over completed finite stages.
- **step-two-towers** [claim](#stmt-thm-two-towers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-two-towers): Quotient normalized middle frames by the determinant-one group and combine commuting torsor presentations. -> The negative-space quotient comparison with actual frame actions.
- **step-etale-section-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-etale-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-etale-descent): Descend idempotents and simple roots by complete finite-stage approximation and root uniqueness. -> Finite étale sections descend from completion to separable stages.
- **step-algebraic-etale-marking** [claim](#stmt-lem-etale-marking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-etale-marking): Descend each finite normalized generator level and use injectivity to retain compatibility and action identities. -> An ordinary algebraic integral Tate marking.
- **step-lift-torsors** [claim](#stmt-lem-lift-torsors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lift-torsors): Base change primitive lift algebras and use the preserved prime basis to identify purely inseparable extensions. -> Honda lift torsors and their compatible root-stage algebras.
- **step-negative-presentation** [claim](#stmt-lem-negative-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-negative-presentation): Use the untilt divisor sequence and finite coefficient-field pushforward, removing zero classes fiberwise. -> The negative space as an affine-line translation quotient.
- **step-finite-resolution** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-resolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-resolution): Construct finite projective resolutions, shorten by duality, and compare continuously with completed bar terms. -> Finite continuous cochain models and the determinant-one dual resolution.
- **step-analytic-roos** [claim](#stmt-lem-analytic-roos) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-analytic-roos): Correct finite solution tuples using density while making corrections tend to zero. -> Surjective Roos difference maps with profinite parameters.
- **step-annulus-restriction** [claim](#stmt-lem-annulus-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-annulus-restriction): Divide nonconstant fractional Laurent coefficients using the radius-gap weight inequalities. -> Restricted nonconstant classes vanish and the Kummer residue survives.
- **step-affine-line** [claim](#stmt-lem-affine-line-flat) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affine-line-flat): Factor disc restrictions through distant-center annuli and trivialize the remaining Kummer class. -> Affine-line cohomology consists only of constants.
- **step-single-hole** [claim](#stmt-lem-single-hole-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-single-hole-residue): Apply the support triangle and bounded exterior primitives; compare Kummer residues after center changes. -> Single-hole support and its pro-zero residue kernel.
- **step-field-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-field-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-field-support): Use disjoint tube covers, parent-child residue sums, and a uniformly pro-zero kernel system. -> Field support is the locally finite distribution module.
- **step-translation-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-lem-translation-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-translation-cohomology): Use lattice restriction for constants and continuous coinduction followed by the completed Koszul resolution for distributions. -> Translation cohomology and its inverse norm orientation.
- **step-geometric-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-prop-geometric-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-geometric-cohomology): Apply translation cohomology to the support triangle and track the lattice orientation. -> The geometric constant and norm-character lines.
- **step-frobenius-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-frobenius-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frobenius-descent): Solve coefficient Artin-Schreier equations on finite clopen parameter partitions while controlling annular tails. -> Exact fixed-stage Frobenius fibers of actual cochain complexes.
- **step-completed-calculation** [claim](#stmt-thm-completed-calculation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-completed-calculation): Compare the fixed-stage quotient stacks, use a bounded finite-coefficient spectral sequence, then shrink marking subgroups. -> Finite completed-stage cohomology and the marking-colimit lines with their actions.
- **step-compact-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-duality): Dualize finite projective Hom terms and reverse the determinant-one resolution. -> Natural compact-discrete cohomology duality with quotient topology.
- **step-strictness** [claim](#stmt-lem-strictness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strictness): Apply Baire's theorem to image closures and lift by a convergent sequence of corrections. -> Open boundaries and small primitives.
- **step-profinite-parameters** [claim](#stmt-lem-profinite-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profinite-parameters): Lift clopen approximations uniformly and use finite discrete cohomology values. -> Cohomology and stage colimits commute with compact profinite parameters.
- **step-distribution-operator** [claim](#stmt-lem-distribution-operator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-distribution-operator): Use Cartier-dual torsion distributions and regular nilpotent blocks, then descend finite matrices and sections. -> The root-detecting distribution operator with finite-stage continuous lifts.
- **step-invariant-differential** [claim](#stmt-prop-invariant-differential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-invariant-differential): Take determinant quotients of Kodaira–Spencer and the invariant conormal frame. -> An invariant differential basis at a finite separable marking stage.
- **step-cartier-traces** [claim](#stmt-lem-cartier-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cartier-traces): Identify equivariant semilinear trace functionals by the perfect Cartier pairing and compute residue adjoints. -> Normalized traces, exact Cartier factors, and residue lattice duality.
- **step-good-stages** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-stages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-stages): Intersect finite-data stabilizers and a finite distribution-jet kernel, then shrink to normal uniform subgroups. -> Good stages stable under the required root-stage distribution action.
- **step-completed-positive** [claim](#stmt-lem-completed-positive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completed-positive): Shrink positive cocycles by Frobenius and apply strictness, then use finite residue coefficients. -> Positive acyclicity and finite completed principal-part cohomology.
- **step-compact-operator** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-operator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-operator): Separate the finite stable image and apply the power-series module structure theorem to the contracting quotient. -> Finite operator kernels, finite-orbit subgroups, and stable-image compatibility.
- **step-finite-stage-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-stage-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-stage-finiteness): Control stable Cartier images by completed principal parts, eliminate a largest uncountable degree, then apply compact duality. -> Finite cohomology at all good finite root stages and invariant valuation-order lattices.
- **step-decompletion** [claim](#stmt-thm-decompletion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-decompletion): Kill positive root-stage cohomology under Frobenius and compare the common discrete quotient before taking marking colimits. -> The natural root-to-completion quasi-isomorphism with full actions.
- **step-continuous-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-continuous-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-continuous-extension): Use continuous sections to compare bar resolutions and form a first-quadrant quotient double complex. -> Continuous extension spectral sequences and comparison descent.
- **step-ordinary-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-thm-coefficient-calculation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-coefficient-calculation): Average the distribution sign sequence over the tame quotient, then descend through the principal norm quotient. -> Ordinary marking cohomology with lower constant and upper norm-character lines.
- **step-spherical-constants** [claim](#stmt-lem-spherical-constants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spherical-constants): Lift finite étale constants, select a Morava factor, and check the coordinate conversion to unextended bar faces. -> Finite free spherical constants and descendable unextended Amitsur terms.
- **step-finite-totalizations** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-totalizations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-totalizations): Prove initiality using contractible labeling complexes and retain augmentation and tower compatibility. -> Exact functors preserve the partial totalization tower.
- **step-tested-descent** [claim](#stmt-prop-descent-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-descent-test): Carry the Amitsur error nilpotence bound through finite totalizations and identify stable tower images with the actual abutment filtration. -> Strongly convergent tested descent with an actual finite diagonal filtration.
- **step-ordinary-cooperations** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordinary-cooperations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-cooperations): Use a finite ordinary Landweber resolution, identify formal-law isomorphisms, and evaluate continuous functions at finite marking stages. -> Even flat cooperation terms and the ordinary residue cochain algebra.
- **step-completed-test-terms** [claim](#stmt-lem-completed-test-terms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completed-test-terms): Apply derived regular-ideal completion and use surjective even homotopy quotient transitions. -> Completed homotopy cochains as inverse limits of jet quotients.
- **step-split-jet-lift** [claim](#stmt-lem-split-jet-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-split-jet-lift): Split over perfect coefficients and lift ring maps by finite Hasse–Taylor substitution and the Kodaira–Spencer torsor isomorphism. -> Unique compatible equivariant split-deformation maps at every jet.
- **step-all-jet-comparison** [claim](#stmt-thm-jet-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-jet-comparison): Recover formal laws from cofinal finite torsion kernels, evaluate ordinary cooperations, compare finite filtrations, and pass to the inverse limit. -> The full realized semilinear action on completed homotopy cochains and their second page.
- **step-generic-character** [claim](#stmt-lem-generic-character) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-generic-character): Show infinite full-unit image, exclude nonconstant fixed Laurent functions, and compare valuations against the intrinsic Hasse section. -> The norm line is excluded from every periodicity power.
- **step-generic-homotopy** [claim](#stmt-prop-generic-homotopy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-generic-homotopy): Use parity and norm-character inequivalence to exclude every possible differential, then read the actual finite filtration. -> Generic homotopy contains the norm-character subrepresentation.
- **step-main-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-generic-homotopy-2): Localize a hypothetical finite assembly, extend finite free constants, and contradict the forced constituent condition. -> Failure of ordinary finite assembly at the specified prime and height.

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-analytic-roos",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-analytic-roos",
          "exact_quote": "Let $$M_0 \\xleftarrow{f_0} M_1 \\xleftarrow{f_1} M_2\n       \\xleftarrow{} \\cdots$$ be a countable inverse system of complete nonarchimedean normed abelian groups. Suppose every $f_n$ is a contraction with dense image. Then the map $$\\prod_{n\\geq0}M_n\\longrightarrow\\prod_{n\\geq0}M_n,\\qquad\n (x_n)\\longmapsto(x_n-f_n(x_{n+1}))\n \\label{eq:analytic-roos}$$ is surjective. The same assertion holds after replacing every $M_n$ by $\\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,M_n)$. Consequently these systems have no positive derived inverse limits.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-analytic-roos",

        "Complete normed inverse system",

      "method": "Correct finite solution tuples using density while making corrections tend to zero.",
      "output": "Surjective Roos difference maps with profinite parameters.",

    },

Suppose $r\\leq r'\\leq b'\\leq b$ and $$r'\\geq r/\\theta,\\qquad b'\\leq b\\theta.\n \\label{eq:analytic-radius-gap}$$ After restriction to $A[r',b']$, every degree-one class represented by a Laurent series with zero constant coefficient vanishes. Division by $\\epsilon^u-1$, for $u\\neq0$, has operator norm at most one between the indicated weighted coefficient spaces. The remaining degree-one class is the Kummer class of $z$, with coefficients in $R_T$.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-geometric-cohomology",
          "exact_quote": "For every compact profinite $T$, the geometric period domain has cohomology $$H^i_v(T\\times(N^\\ast)_{C^\\flat},\\mathcal O^\\flat)\n =\n \\begin{cases}\n R_T,&i=0,\\\\\n R_T\\otimes_{C^\\flat}\\ell_C,&i=3,\\\\\n 0,&i\\neq0,3,\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:analytic-geometric-rows}$$ where $\\ell_C$ is a one-dimensional $C^\\flat$-space. All identifications are natural under continuous maps of $T$ and the frame-group actions. On $\\ell_C$, $\\mathcal O_F^\\times\\subset J$ acts through the nontrivial character $$\\delta(u)=N_{F/\\mathbb Q_p}(u)\\bmod3.$$ The kernel-scalar action of $\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$ is trivial. The line spaces in <a href=\"#eq:analytic-geometric-rows\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:analytic-geometric-rows\">[eq:analytic-geometric-rows]</a> have their usual topology, as expressed by the continuous-parameter formula.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a finite product of finite separable extensions of $k((t))$. For every compact profinite $Q$ there is an exact sequence $$0\\longrightarrow\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(Q,B^\\flat)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(Q,\\mathcal A_B)\n \\xrightarrow{\\Phi-1}\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(Q,\\mathcal A_B)\n \\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:analytic-frobenius-exact}$$ It is natural in $B,Q$ and continuous actions over $k$. In particular, for a compact group $G$ acting continuously on $B$, over $k$ and preserving its valuations, the natural map identifies actual cochain complexes by $$C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,B^\\flat;T)\n \\simeq\n \\operatorname{fib}\\left(\n \\Phi-1:C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,\\mathcal A_B;T)\n               \\longrightarrow C^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(G,\\mathcal A_B;T)\\right).\n \\label{eq:analytic-frobenius-cochains}$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-completed-calculation",
          "exact_quote": "For a cofinal family of sufficiently small open normal subgroups $U\\subset J$, put $B=L^U$. Then $H^a_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\\flat)$ is finite for every $a$, and it is zero for $a>7$. For every compact profinite $T$, evaluation induces the natural identification $$H^aC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\\flat;T)\n =\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}\n       \\bigl(T,H^a_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,B^\\flat)\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:analytic-finite-stage-parameters}$$ where the finite group on the right is discrete. Moreover, $$(B^\\flat)^H=k,\\qquad L^H=k,\n \\label{eq:analytic-invariant-field}$$ and $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{colim}}_U H^aC^\\bullet_{\\mathrm{cts}}(H,(L^U)^\\flat;T)\n =\n \\begin{cases}\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,k),&a=0,\\\\\n \\operatorname{Map}_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,\\ell),&a=3,\\\\\n 0,&a\\neq0,3.\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:analytic-completed-colimit}$$ The colimit identifications respect restrictions, conjugations and the $J$-action. The residual $P/H=\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$-action on both lines is trivial; on the upper line $\\mathcal O_F^\\times\\subset J$ acts by $\\delta$. The statements are natural in $T$.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-profinite-parameters",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be an arbitrary compact profinite space.*\n\n1.  *A continuous map from $T$ to $F$ lifts across an open continuous surjection $E\\twoheadrightarrow F$ of complete metrizable linearly topologized groups.*\n\n\n2.  *If a complex $A^\\bullet$ of complete, separable, metrizable, linearly topologized $\\mathbb F_p$-spaces with continuous linear differentials has finite cohomology, then naturally $$\\label{eq:finite-cohomology-parameters}\n     H^a C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,A^\\bullet)\n     \\cong C_{\\mathrm{cts}}(T,H^a(A^\\bullet)),$$ where the group on the right is discrete.*\n\n3.  *For a filtered diagram of such complexes, this identity commutes with the cochain colimit, with the colimit of cohomology groups given the discrete topology.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-distribution-operator",
          "exact_quote": "After the fixed finite enlargement of $k$, the inverse-limit distribution algebra of the translation tower is $k[[D]]$. A parameter $D$ can be chosen with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *Its action on $R$ is $L$-linear and locally nilpotent, commutes with $H$, and satisfies $$\\label{eq:distribution-kernels}\n     \\ker(D^{p^i}:R\\to R)=L^{1/p^i}\\quad(i\\ge0),\n     \\qquad D:R\\twoheadrightarrow R.$$*\n\n2.  *The order-two norm quotient acts on $D$ by its nontrivial character. The action of $J$ on $k[[D]]$ is continuous on finite jets.*\n\n\n3.  *If $i$ is positive, even, and divisible by $[k:\\mathbb F_p]$, and $q=p^i$, then $$\\label{eq:distribution-frobenius}\n     D(f^q)=(D^qf)^q,\\qquad\n     D^q(af)=aD^qf\\quad(a\\in L^{1/q}).$$*\n\n4.  *Each operator on a fixed root stage is continuous after passage to a finite larger marking stage. Every finite target stage has a continuous additive lift under $D$ at some finite larger marking and root stage. Thus the sequence with kernel $L$ and operator $D$ is exact on the prescribed cochains, also with profinite parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-cartier-traces",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-distribution-operator",
        "step-invariant-differential",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cartier-traces",
          "exact_quote": "For every $i\\ge1$, with $q=p^i$, there is $c_i\\in k^\\times$ such that $$\\label{eq:Cartier-distribution-trace}\n D^{q-1}(f^{1/q})=c_i C_0^i(f)\\qquad(f\\in L).$$ In particular, $C_0(f^p)=0$. If $B=L^U$ contains $\\eta$ as a basis, then $$\\label{eq:Cartier-exact-sequence}\n 0\\longrightarrow B\\xrightarrow{\\mathrm{Fr}}B\n \\xrightarrow{d/\\eta}B\\xrightarrow{C_0}B\\longrightarrow0$$ is exact and its two short exact factors have continuous additive sections onto their images.*\n\n\n\n*Write $B=\\prod_j\\kappa_j((s_j))$ and $\\eta=h_j(s_j)\\,ds_j/s_j$ on its factors. Define $$M=\\{f:\\operatorname{ord}_{s_j}(f_jh_j)>0\\text{ for all }j\\},\n \\qquad\n M^0=\\{f:\\operatorname{ord}_{s_j}(f_jh_j)\\ge0\\text{ for all }j\\}.$$ These are compact open $H$- and $C_0$-stable lattices, and $M^0/M$ is finite. For the pairing $$\\label{eq:residue-pairing}\n \\langle f,g\\rangle_B\n =\\sum_j\\operatorname{Tr}_{\\kappa_j/\\mathbb F_p}\n             \\operatorname{Res}_{s_j}(f_jg_j\\eta)$$ there are topological $H$-equivariant identifications $$\\label{eq:residue-lattice-duals}\n M^\\vee=B/\\mathcal O_B,\\qquad\n (B/M)^\\vee=\\mathcal O_B,\\qquad\n \\langle C_0f,g\\rangle_B=\\langle f,g^p\\rangle_B.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-318.4"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-operator",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be a compact Hausdorff $\\mathbb F_p$-vector space, let $C:N\\to N$ be continuous and $\\mathbb F_p$-linear, and suppose that $$I=\\bigcap_{n\\ge0}C^nN\n \\quad\\text{and}\\quad N/CN$$ are finite. If $f:N\\to X$ is a homomorphism with finite kernel, then $$\\label{eq:stable-image-finite-fiber}\n \\bigcap_{n\\ge0}f(C^nN)=f(I).$$ No topology on $X$ is required in this last assertion.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-finite-assembly-obstruction**: At prime three and height three, the chromatic overlap cannot be obtained from the specified lower local spheres by ordinary thick closure.
- **invariant-constituent-restriction**: Ordinary thick generation by the local unit forces finite-dimensional generic test representations whose simple constituents are periodicity powers in every degree.
- **invariant-norm-character-exclusion**: The generic deformation field has only the constant fixed field under the full unit group, and the norm-character line is inequivalent to every periodicity power.
- **invariant-actual-norm-subrepresentation**: The generic diagonal spectral sequence collapses at its second page, and its actual finite abutment filtration contains the norm-character subrepresentation in degree minus three.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-extended-closure**: Apply the obstruction to constructions allowing arbitrary tensor factors, infinite coproducts, or unrestricted homotopy limits. -> Those operations enlarge the closure beyond the category tested by the constituent obstruction.
- **anti-unit-retraction**: Treat failure of finite assembly as a proof that the canonical localization unit has no retraction. -> The finite-assembly detector does not track a distinguished unit map.
- **anti-residue-only-character-test**: Replace the full unit action by its finite residue-field subgroup when testing whether the norm line is a periodicity power. -> The restriction loses the principal-unit action needed to establish the generic fixed field and exclude a nonconstant change of basis.
- **anti-closed-fiber-comparison**: Infer the generic stabilizer action from a comparison only at the closed deformation fiber. -> A closed-fiber identification does not determine the action after inverting the deformation parameter.
- **anti-split-abutment**: Replace the equivariant homotopy extensions by direct sums of their graded constituents. -> The filtration identifies subrepresentations and quotients without proving an equivariant splitting.
- **anti-prime-height-generalisation**: Extend the negative finite-assembly verdict to other primes or chromatic heights. -> The main theorem fixes the exceptional prime and height; the source also describes positive finite constructions in a different prime range.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is a structural nonmembership theorem for localized spectra, rather than a finite constructive computation. The constituent restriction ranges over every integer degree and periodicity power. The proof passes through finite-stage coefficient colimits, completed perfections, comparisons through every deformation jet, and an actual homotopy filtration. Finite enumeration of residue characters or truncated jets would not certify this obstruction. The source expressly states: "The following lemma uses the whole group, including its principal units."

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite-assembly obstruction concerns localized module spectra and their ordinary thick closure. It supplies no executable software construction or runtime contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The geometric period domain is studied through sheaf cohomology and frame-group actions. These constructions specify neither a game state model nor a simulation update rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The comparison through every deformation jet is an abstract cochain quasi-isomorphism. The paper does not supply an implemented symbolic procedure or a machine-checkable proof artifact. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Existence of a finite projective resolution does not specify an algorithm for producing its projective modules, differentials, and continuous comparison homotopies from encoded input. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Locally finite distributions are defined using inverse limits of unrestricted products with product topologies. This is a mathematical coefficient module rather than a finite storage representation with access and update operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The completed homotopy terms are inverse limits of cooperation quotients tensored with periodicity lines. Their description supplies no finite hardware workload, arithmetic layout, or resource estimate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The residue norm character distinguishes semilinear representation lines over the generic deformation field. It is not accompanied by a cryptographic primitive, security game, or hardness assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The constituent test constrains algebraic semilinear representations produced by finite constructions of spectra. It provides no learned representation, training objective, or statistical inference procedure. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact-operator lemma proves finiteness under hypotheses on stable images and quotients. It gives neither an optimisation objective nor a method for searching a feasible set. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Continuous extension descent compares cochains for an extension of compact profinite groups. Its section and parameter hypotheses concern coefficient topology rather than communication, process failures, or consensus. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper constructs compatible deformation maps and isomorphisms by square-zero lifting and uniqueness. These are mathematical constructions over marking and perfection coefficients, without a finite effective engineering input and output contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-exceptional-prime-height** (counterexample_scope): The finite-assembly obstruction applies at the stated prime and height, for the derived complete sphere in the specified localized module category. Blocked use: ['Extending the negative verdict to arbitrary primes, heights, or other ambient categories.']
- **limit-ordinary-finite-closure** (formal_scope): The permitted closure is ordinary thick closure, rather than closure under arbitrary tensor factors or infinite constructions. Blocked use: ['Using the constituent obstruction for thick tensor ideals, infinite coproducts, or unrestricted homotopy limits.']
- **limit-distinguished-unit** (formal_scope): Failure of finite assembly does not establish that the canonical localization unit fails to retract. Blocked use: ['Reporting the main theorem as a counterexample to the separate canonical-unit retraction assertion.']
- **limit-detector-antecedent** (hypothesis): Finite dimensionality and the periodicity constituent restriction follow from ordinary thick generation by the local unit. Blocked use: ['Assuming the detector is finite-dimensional for an arbitrary spectrum.', 'Inferring thick membership merely from satisfaction of the necessary constituent condition.']
- **limit-finite-torsion-before-specialization** (formal_scope): The finite torsion system must retain the component that becomes étale after specialization; completion at the identity after specialization loses that component. Blocked use: ['Replacing the algebraic specialized group by its formal completion when constructing integral étale markings and generator-lift torsors.']
- **limit-stage-cochain-convention** (formal_scope): Incomplete coefficient cochains have values in a single complete finite marking or root stage. Completion of the whole tower is not an interchangeable coefficient convention. Blocked use: ['Replacing the prescribed degreewise stage colimit by all continuous maps into an incompletely topologized union.', 'Using the geometric completion of the whole tower as ordinary cochain coefficients without a comparison theorem.']
- **limit-integral-marking-stage-control** (formal_scope): Each finite torsion level of the normalized étale marking descends to a finite separable stage; the entire compatible marking need not occur at a single finite stage. Blocked use: ['Demanding a common finite separable stage for the entire Tate generator.', 'Treating a generator-lift algebra as the algebra of the entire height-three torsion group.']
- **limit-analytic-resolution-topology** (hypothesis): The finite analytic resolution requires absence of elements of order the coefficient characteristic. Its comparison with continuous cochains requires complete coefficients with invariant neighborhoods. Blocked use: ['Applying the resolution theorem to analytic groups with the excluded torsion.', 'Applying its continuous comparison to incomplete coefficients or actions without the stated neighborhood condition.', 'Reading resolution length as a software runtime bound.']
- **limit-countable-dense-contractions** (hypothesis): The Roos surjectivity argument uses a countable system of complete nonarchimedean normed groups with dense contracting transitions. Blocked use: ['Transferring derived-limit vanishing to arbitrary inverse systems.', 'Treating a merely dense transition as sufficient without completeness and contraction.']
- **limit-annular-restriction-gap** (approximation): Nonconstant annular classes vanish after the prescribed restriction. This does not assert that a fixed annulus has only its constant degree-one class. Blocked use: ['Discarding nonconstant cohomology on a fixed closed annulus.', 'Dividing Laurent coefficients without verifying the required inner and outer radius gaps.']
- **limit-distribution-product-topology** (formal_scope): Locally finite distributions use product topology on the coset coefficient modules and summation under refinement. Blocked use: ['Imposing an additional uniform boundedness condition across the discrete coset index.', 'Replacing the distribution inverse limit by a finite truncation and claiming the same support calculation.']
- **limit-strictness-and-profinite-families** (hypothesis): Strictness uses complete, separable, metrizable, linearly topologized coefficient complexes with finite cohomology. The parameter space itself may be an arbitrary compact profinite space. Blocked use: ['Invoking small primitives before finite cohomology and the coefficient topology have been established.', 'Assuming arbitrary parameterized cochain spaces are metrizable or countable.']
- **limit-good-stage-finiteness** (hypothesis): Finite root-stage cohomology is proved at good marking stages and for invariant valuation-order lattices in those stages. Blocked use: ['Asserting finite cohomology for arbitrary incomplete coefficient unions or arbitrary lattices.', 'Removing good-stage control from the decompletion argument.']
- **limit-distribution-frobenius-regime** (hypothesis): The distribution Frobenius identities require the stated positive even iterate compatible with the finite constant field. Continuous lifts are obtained after passage to sufficiently large finite stages. Blocked use: ['Applying the Frobenius identities at arbitrary iterates.', 'Assuming every distribution preimage remains in the original coefficient stage.']
- **limit-continuous-extension-section** (hypothesis): Continuous extension descent requires a continuous section of the quotient map as a map of spaces and the stipulated kernel comparison with all compact profinite parameters. Blocked use: ['Using a comparison only for a point to infer the full-group comparison.', 'Applying the continuous extension statement without its section and coefficient-stage hypotheses.']
- **limit-exactness-and-actual-abutment** (formal_scope): The tested descent argument transports finite partial totalizations and a uniform error-tower bound through the exact test. A surviving page term becomes a homotopy constituent through the actual finite abutment filtration. Blocked use: ['Assuming exactness alone preserves arbitrary inverse limits.', 'Identifying a spectral-sequence page constituent with actual homotopy without convergence and filtration control.']
- **limit-every-jet-and-finite-coefficient-sets** (formal_scope): The generic action requires compatible comparisons through every deformation jet. Finite-stage control applies to each finite set of formal-isomorphism coefficients. Blocked use: ['Inferring the generic semilinear action from the closed fiber or finitely many jets.', 'Requiring one finite coefficient stage for the entire formal-group isomorphism.']
- **limit-full-unit-character-action** (hypothesis): The exclusion of the norm line from periodicity powers uses the full unit group, including its principal units, acting semilinearly on the generic deformation field. Blocked use: ['Replacing the full unit action by its finite residue subgroup.', 'Ignoring the scalar action when comparing character lines.']
- **limit-unsplit-homotopy-extensions** (formal_scope): The generic homotopy calculation supplies equivariant subrepresentations and quotients, without asserting that the extensions split. Blocked use: ['Replacing the actual equivariant extensions by direct sums of their graded pieces.']
- **limit-no-finite-execution-certificate** (nonconstructive): The structural nonmembership proof uses complete coefficient stages, infinite compatible comparisons, and a contradiction from actual semilinear constituents. No finite executable decision procedure or complexity contract is supplied. Blocked use: ['Presenting finite residue-character enumeration or truncated-jet agreement as certification of the main theorem.', 'Reporting an executable refutation oracle or implementation complexity derived from this proof.']

Restriction induces $$\\label{eq:finite-totalization-cube}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tot}}_r X^\\bullet=\\lim_{\\Delta_{\\leq r}}X^\\bullet\n \\simeq\\lim_{S\\in\\mathcal P_0([r])}X^{|S|-1}.$$ These equivalences respect the totalization tower and the augmentation when $X^\\bullet$ is augmented. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


At the prime three and height three, the chromatic overlap cannot be constructed from the rational, height-one, and height-two local spheres by finitely many sums, shifts, cofibers, and retracts in the category of $`E(2)`$-local modules over the derived 3-complete sphere. This gives a negative answer to the ordinary finite-assembly question for chromatic overlaps.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Chromatic fracture reconstructs a spectrum from its local pieces and the maps relating adjacent heights. For the sphere, one of the objects that carries this gluing information is the overlap $$L_{n-1}L_{K(n)}S.$$ Here $S=S_p^\wedge$ is the derived $p$-complete sphere, $L_i$ denotes localization with respect to Johnson–Wilson theory $E(i)$, $L_0$ is rationalization, and $L_{K(n)}$ denotes Morava $K(n)$-localization. Classical chromatic splitting asks for a precise description of this overlap by lower local spheres (Hovey 1995; Barthel and Beaudry 2020) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Hovey93](#ref-Hovey93), [BB19](#ref-BB19)]. We study the less restrictive question of whether those spheres can produce the overlap by any finite construction.

<a id="eq:finite-assembly-question"></a>
For objects $X_1,\ldots,X_r$ in the category of $E(n-1)$-local $S$-modules, let $\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}\{X_1,\ldots,X_r\}$ be the smallest full subcategory containing them and closed under equivalences, finite sums, integer suspensions and desuspensions, homotopy cofibers, and retracts (Hovey and Strickland 1999, Definitions 1.2, 1.3(c), and 1.5(c)) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HS99](#ref-HS99)]. Every map is $S$-linear. This is the *ordinary* thick subcategory: tensoring with an arbitrary module, taking an infinite coproduct, or taking an unrestricted homotopy limit is not an additional closure operation. The finite-assembly question asks whether $$\label{eq:finite-assembly-question}
 L_{n-1}L_{K(n)}S\in
       \mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}\{L_0S,L_1S,\ldots,L_{n-1}S\}$$ for every prime $p$ and every $n\geq1$. The number of cofibers and their attaching maps may depend on $p$ and $n$. In particular, this question allows nonsplit extensions and Moore corrections: a cofiber of multiplication by a power of $p$ is one of the permitted constructions.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *At $p=n=3$, with $S=S_3^\wedge$, $$L_2L_{K(3)}S
                  \notin\mathop{\mathrm{Thick}}\{L_0S,L_1S,L_2S\}$$ in the category of $E(2)$-local $S$-modules.*

</div>

This disproves the ordinary finite-assembly statement <a href="#eq:finite-assembly-question" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-assembly-question">[eq:finite-assembly-question]</a>. Its scope is the specified prime and height. The obstruction does not depend on a proposed list of summands or on a bound for the number of allowed cofibers.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Splittings, finite constructions, and the prime three

The historical splitting assertions contain more data than ordinary thick membership. In Hovey’s account of Hopkins’ conjecture, the proposed exterior classes, factorizations, and summands are distinguished from the splitting of the canonical cofiber sequence (Hovey 1995, Conjecture 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Hovey93](#ref-Hovey93)]. The detection result there is conditional on chromatic splitting (Hovey 1995, Theorem 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Hovey93](#ref-Hovey93)]. For the sphere, the weak splitting question asks only whether the canonical unit $$L_{n-1}S\longrightarrow L_{n-1}L_{K(n)}S$$ has a retraction; the finite-input formulation is stated in Beaudry, Goerss, and Henn (2022, Conjecture 1.1.11) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BGH2022](#ref-BGH2022)]. Ordinary thick membership records no distinguished map at all. A prescribed strong decomposition with its unit would give both a finite construction and a weak splitting, but a finite construction may have nonsplit attachments and need not split that unit.

At height two, the positive odd-prime results developed from Shimomura–Yabe’s calculation for $p\geq5$ (Shimomura and Yabe 1995, Theorem 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ShimomuraYabe95](#ref-ShimomuraYabe95)], revisited and corrected by Behrens (2012, Theorem 7.7 and Remark 7.8) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Behrens12](#ref-Behrens12)]. At the prime three, Goerss–Henn–Mahowald established the prescribed height-two splitting (Goerss, Henn, and Mahowald 2014, Theorem 5.11) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[GHM14](#ref-GHM14)]. Thus prime three admits a height-two splitting, while Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> obstructs even finite assembly at the next height. The prime-two case explains why failure of a prescribed list need not obstruct finite assembly. Beaudry disproved the original strong wedge formula at $n=p=2$ (Beaudry 2017, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Beaudry2017](#ref-Beaudry2017)]. Beaudry–Goerss–Henn then described the additional Moore-spectrum terms while retaining the canonical unit retraction (Beaudry, Goerss, and Henn 2022, Theorem 1.1.6) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BGH2022](#ref-BGH2022)]. Those extra terms are allowed by the ordinary thick closure above. Thus the known failure of a prescribed wedge is not by itself an obstruction to finite assembly. Conversely, Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> addresses the finite-construction question, not the separate assertion that a particular canonical unit fails to retract. The revised strong summand patterns and their known low-height cases are discussed in Barthel and Beaudry (2020, sec. 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BB19](#ref-BB19)].

The companion article *Filtered chromatic splitting at generic primes* constructs an actual filtration by lower local spheres for $n\geq1$ and $p>n+1$ (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[CompanionFiltered](#ref-CompanionFiltered)]. Its prime range excludes the example studied here. The companion rational obstruction at height three concerns a prescribed strong wedge for $p\geq5$ (OpenAI 2026a, Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[CompanionRational](#ref-CompanionRational)]; such an obstruction is compatible with a finite construction by nonsplit cofibers. Neither companion result supplies a step in the prime-three proof below.

The connected-marking method has earlier coheight-one precedents. Torii’s Laurent-field coefficient tower builds on Gross’s monodromy calculation and carries commuting stabilizer actions from the two heights (Gross 1979, Theorem 3.5(1a)) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Gross79](#ref-Gross79)] <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Torii03](#ref-Torii03)] (Torii 2003, Theorems 2.7 and 2.9 and Corollary 2.8). Torii later constructed a localized Adams spectral sequence and detected the degree-minus-one reduced-norm class in actual iterated localization (Torii 2011, Theorems 4.7 and 8.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Torii11](#ref-Torii11)]. The present obstruction requires further comparisons that retain the exceptional norm character through ordinary finite-stage coefficients and the full lower-height deformation.

The geometric starting point is the coheight-one work of Barthel et al. (2026) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BMR26](#ref-BMR26)]. For odd $p$ and $h\geq2$, their theorem has an exceptional branch when $p-1$ divides $h-1$ (Barthel et al. 2026, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[BMR26](#ref-BMR26)]. The calculation of the completed orientation line assumes $k\supseteq\mathbb F_{p^{h(h-1)}}$. At $p=h=3$, with our choice $k\supseteq\mathbb F_{3^6}$, the tame determinant invariants retain a second cohomology line with a nontrivial lower-height norm character (Barthel et al. 2026, Theorem D, Theorems 3.6.10 and 5.2.10, and Lemma 5.2.5) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[BMR26](#ref-BMR26)]. Their completed proxy does not by itself identify the ordinary overlap used here; in particular, an invertible cofiber for the proxy does not decide whether its attachment splits (Barthel et al. 2026, Theorem A and Remark 5.2.12) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[BMR26](#ref-BMR26)]. The shifted orientation line has a representation-theoretic antecedent in Heyer’s derived Steinberg coinvariant calculation (Heyer 2023, Corollary 5.3.4) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Heyer23](#ref-Heyer23)] <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[HeyerGeo24](#ref-HeyerGeo24)] (Heyer 2024, Lemma 4.1.6 and Corollary 4.3.10), used geometrically in Barthel et al. (2026, secs. 3.5–3.6) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BMR26](#ref-BMR26)]. Section <a href="#sec:analytic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic">6</a> computes the required distribution orientation with the coefficient topology used here. The proof below must carry the character from finite marking coefficients to ordinary homotopy and retain the whole height-two deformation along the way. These are the two places where a closed-fiber geometric calculation alone is insufficient.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### A detector that is stable under finite construction

The final obstruction can be stated before its coefficient calculation. Choose a finite field $k$ containing $\mathbb F_{3^6}$, enlarged by a finite extension when needed to fix the constant identifications, and let $E_2=E_2(k)$ be height-two Morava $E$-theory. Its degree-zero ring is $W(k)[[x]]$; the variable $x$ is the height-two deformation parameter. Write $\omega=\pi_2E_2$ for the periodicity line and $$Q_x=k((x)),\qquad
       \overline\omega=(\omega/3)\otimes_{k[[x]]}Q_x.$$ An unramified quadratic field $F/\mathbb Q_3$ lies in the height-two endomorphism algebra. Its unit group $G_F=\mathcal O_F^\times$ acts on $Q_x$ and on $\overline\omega$. The action is semilinear: it acts on scalars as well as on vectors.

For a spectrum $X$, apply the exact test $$\mathcal T(X)=L_{K(2)}(E_2\wedge X)/3,
 \qquad
 \mathcal V_d(X)=\pi_d\mathcal T(X)\otimes_{k[[x]]}Q_x.$$ The smash product here is the ordinary smash product of spectra. After $K(2)$-localization the three proposed generators become $0,0,L_{K(2)}S$, and the test of the last one has one periodicity line in each even degree. Exactness now gives a stringent necessary condition: if $X$ comes from the local unit by the permitted finite operations, then every $\mathcal V_d(X)$ is finite-dimensional and every simple constituent is a power of $\overline\omega$. Proposition <a href="#prop:constituent-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:constituent-test">3</a> proves this by placing those representations in a Serre subcategory. Finite dimensionality is a consequence on the thick closure, not an assumption about the overlap.

The coefficient calculation produces a different line, <a id="eq:intro-norm-line"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-norm-line}
 Q_x(\delta),\qquad
 \delta(g)=\overline{N_{F/\mathbb Q_3}(g)}
       \in\mathbb F_3^\times,\quad g\in G_F.$$ The notation means that $Q_x$ has its usual semilinear action and a chosen basis is multiplied by the constant $\delta(g)$. The character is nontrivial, since the residue norm $\mathbb F_9^\times\to\mathbb F_3^\times$ is surjective. What matters is not merely this residue character but its action over the field $Q_x$. The full group $G_F$, including its principal units, has infinite image on $Q_x$ and fixed field $k$. Together with the first Hasse invariant, this full action excludes the norm line from every periodicity power; Lemma <a href="#lem:generic-character" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:generic-character">50</a> gives the argument.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### From finite markings to actual homotopy

The coefficient calculation and its passage to homotopy solve different problems. The first produces the norm line in ordinary continuous cohomology. The second retains that line through the entire height-two deformation and the filtration of actual homotopy. We describe the interfaces between these arguments before constructing their coefficients.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Integral markings.

On the height-two stratum of the height-three deformation, the algebraic $p$-divisible group has a connected part of height two and an étale quotient of height one. Its finite torsion kernels are formed before passing to the stratum, so both parts are retained. Over the one-parameter field $K=k((t))$, let $L^U$ be the finite étale algebra of connected markings modulo an open subgroup $U$ of the height-two stabilizer $J$. Put $$L=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{U\subset J}L^U,\qquad R=L^{\mathrm{perf}}.$$ The groups $P$ and $J$ are the unextended height-three and height-two stabilizers, respectively; both fix $k$. The group $P$ transports the deformation, commuting with the marking action of $J$. Section <a href="#sec:towers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:towers">4</a> constructs a determinant-normalized integral marking of the étale quotient. The normalization determines the norm action on the coefficients. Relative section-sheaf full faithfulness (Anschütz and Le Bras 2025, Corollary 3.11) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[ALB25](#ref-ALB25)] makes this a construction of families of markings, including their frame changes.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Ordinary cohomology.

The geometry first computes cohomology with the completed perfection of each finite stage $L^U$. Returning to ordinary coefficients is a separate argument: a cochain with values in $L$ or $R$ must have its image in one complete finite marking or root stage. The central step in Section <a href="#sec:decompletion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:decompletion">7</a> proves finite cohomology at such stages. It uses an invariant differential to normalize Cartier’s operator, whose residue adjoint is Frobenius (Cartier 1958, chap. II, Section 6) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Cartier58](#ref-Cartier58)] <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Serre58Topology](#ref-Serre58Topology)] (Serre 1958, Proposition 9). This finiteness controls primitives of small cocycles and allows passage from root stages to completed perfections, including continuous profinite families. A distribution operator then removes the roots after tame sign averaging. Descent along the remaining norm quotient gives Theorem <a href="#thm:coefficient-calculation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:coefficient-calculation">41</a>: the ordinary $P$-cohomology of $L$ has four lines, in degrees $0,1,3,4$. The upper two carry the character $\delta$ on $G_F\subset J$.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Finite descent in spectra.

To place this calculation in homotopy, first extend constants by a finite étale $S$-algebra $S_k$, whose underlying $S$-module is a finite sum of copies of $S$, and put $Z_k=L_{K(3)}S_k$. Morava descent expresses $Z_k$ as the totalization of a cosimplicial spectrum. Descendability (Mathew 2016) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Mathew16](#ref-Mathew16)] bounds the composites in its error tower; exactness of $\mathcal T$ preserves this bound. Thus the tested descent spectral sequence has a finite filtration on its actual abutment $\pi_*\mathcal T(Z_k)$, as proved in Proposition <a href="#prop:descent-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:descent-test">44</a>. On mod-$3$ homotopy, a diagonal summand aligns the constants from $S_k$ and $E_2$. Descendability uses a thick tensor ideal; the finite-assembly detector uses the ordinary thick closure of Section <a href="#sec:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:detector">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### The whole height-two deformation.

The homotopy cochain terms of that spectral sequence must still be compared with the ordinary coefficient calculation. Section <a href="#sec:homotopy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:homotopy">8</a> starts with ordinary, unlocalized cooperations and expresses the completed terms as inverse limits of their quotients by $x^m$. Over the perfect ring $R$, the marked connected–étale extension splits. Square-zero deformation torsors and the universal Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism (Lau 2010, sec. 5, especially Theorem 5.1 and the paragraph following Equation (5.2)) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Lau10](#ref-Lau10)] lift this splitting through every ring $R[x]/(x^m)$. Theorem <a href="#thm:jet-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:jet-comparison">48</a> recovers the formal-law isomorphism from the full torsion system and uses it to evaluate ordinary cooperations. Finite Taylor expansion keeps each evaluation in one finite coefficient stage. The resulting compatible cochain maps retain the realized $J$-action through every jet and hence on completed homotopy.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
###### The surviving constituent.

After inverting $x$, the four rows are periodicity lines in degrees $0,1$ and their norm twists in degrees $3,4$. The only possible higher differentials go from a lower line to an upper line. The full-unit inequivalence in Lemma <a href="#lem:generic-character" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:generic-character">50</a> makes them zero. Together with the finite abutment filtration, this computes the generic diagonal test in every degree (Proposition <a href="#prop:generic-homotopy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:generic-homotopy">51</a>): it has dimension two, with one periodicity constituent and one norm-twisted constituent. In particular, the term of bidegree $(s,t)=(3,0)$ gives $Q_x(\delta)$ in total degree $-3$. Since $S_k$ is finite free over $S$, finite assembly of the original overlap would impose the constituent condition on $\mathcal V_{-3}(Z_k)$ as well. The norm line violates that condition.

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-chain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-chain">1</a> separates the two coefficient comparisons from the descent and representation-theoretic inputs that let their output survive in actual homotopy.

<figure id="fig:proof-chain">

<figcaption>The coefficient comparisons and their passage to homotopy. The full-unit action rules out differentials between the four generic rows; the finite abutment filtration places their constituents in actual homotopy. The finite-assembly detector then excludes the norm constituent. Arrows indicate inputs to the next displayed stage.</figcaption>
</figure>

Section <a href="#sec:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:detector">2</a> proves the finite-construction test, and Sections <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">3</a>–<a href="#sec:towers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:towers">4</a> prepare the stage coefficients and markings. Section <a href="#sec:finite-resolutions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite-resolutions">5</a> supplies the finite analytic resolution needed in Sections <a href="#sec:analytic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic">6</a>–<a href="#sec:decompletion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:decompletion">7</a>. Section <a href="#sec:homotopy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:homotopy">8</a> proves convergence and identifies the ordinary cooperations, which Section <a href="#sec:jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:jets">9</a> compares through every jet. Section <a href="#sec:obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:obstruction">10</a> completes the argument.

<a id="sec:detector"></a>
### The finite-assembly detector

We first isolate the representation-theoretic condition imposed by a finite construction from the $K(2)$-local unit. This condition will later be tested on the abutment of the descent spectral sequence.

Throughout the proof, $p=3$. Fix the finite field $k$ chosen in the introduction. Let $\Gamma_i$ be the one-dimensional Honda formal group of height $i$, in a coordinate with $p$-series $T^{p^i}$, and put $$D_i=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\Gamma_i)[1/p],\qquad
 P_i=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\Gamma_i)=\mathcal O_{D_i}^{\times},\qquad J=P_2.$$ The Honda endomorphism-order description gives the displayed unit group (Beaudry et al. 2022, Example 2.15) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[BGHS22](#ref-BGHS22)]. These are the unextended stabilizer groups, so their actions fix $k$. Choose the unramified quadratic subfield $F\subset D_2$, and set $$G_F=\mathcal O_F^\times,\quad
 \mathcal O_x=k[[x]],\quad Q_x=k((x)),\quad
 \mathbf 1_2=L_{K(2)}S.$$ The symbol $G_F$ denotes this unit group, not an absolute Galois group. Its action on $\mathcal O_x$ extends to $Q_x$.

Let $E_2=E_2(k)$, with $\pi_0E_2=W(k)[[x]]$. The marked-lift stabilizer action on its coefficients is described in Devinatz and Hopkins (1995, Theorem 1.1 and Equation (1.4)) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[DH95](#ref-DH95)], and its Morava $E$-theory realization is supplied by Goerss and Hopkins (2004, Corollaries 7.6–7.7) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[GH04](#ref-GH04)]. We use the cotangent convention for its degree-two periodicity line, dual to the degree-minus-two tangent convention in those sources: $$\omega=\pi_2E_2,\qquad
 \Omega=\omega/p,\qquad
 \overline\omega=\Omega\otimes_{\mathcal O_x}Q_x.$$ Thus $\pi_{2j}E_2=\omega^{\otimes j}$ and odd homotopy vanishes. A negative tensor power of a line means the corresponding power of its dual. All these lines have their semilinear $J$-action. Unless a relative product is explicitly indicated, $\wedge$ denotes the ordinary smash product of spectra.

<a id="stmt-lem-mod-p-nullity"></a>
<div id="lem:mod-p-nullity" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Reduction modulo $p$). *Multiplication by $p$ is null as an $E_2$-module map on $E_2/p$. For every spectrum $X$, it therefore annihilates the homotopy groups of $L_{K(2)}(E_2\wedge X)/p$. Those groups are naturally $\mathcal O_x$-modules.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-mod-p-nullity"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-mod-p-nullity</summary>

*Proof.* Regularity of $p$ and evenness of $E_2$ give $\pi_1(E_2/p)=0$. Apply $[-,E_2/p]_{E_2}$ to the cofiber sequence for multiplication by $p$ on $E_2$. Restriction along $E_2\to E_2/p$ gives an injection $$[E_2/p,E_2/p]_{E_2}\hookrightarrow\pi_0(E_2/p).$$ The class $p\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$ maps to zero and is therefore zero. Tensoring this null map with $X$ and applying exact localization proves the assertion. A chosen $J$-equivariant nullhomotopy is not needed: the conclusion is the nullity of the module map and the induced action on homotopy. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:generic-test"></a>
For a spectrum $X$, define $$\label{eq:generic-test}
 \mathcal T(X)=L_{K(2)}(E_2\wedge X)/p,
 \qquad
 \mathcal V_d(X)=\pi_d\mathcal T(X)\otimes_{\mathcal O_x}Q_x.$$ The action comes from $E_2$, so every map of underlying spectra induces an equivariant map on this test. The action of $G_F$ is semilinear: $g(av)=g(a)g(v)$ for $a\in Q_x$ and $v\in\mathcal V_d(X)$. For a character $\chi:G_F\to k^\times$, let $Q_x(\chi)$ denote the semilinear line with a basis $e_\chi$ satisfying $g(e_\chi)=\chi(g)e_\chi$.

We use these representations algebraically. A subrepresentation is a $G_F$-stable $Q_x$-linear subspace; no additional continuity condition is imposed on that subspace. Every finite-dimensional semilinear representation has finite length, because a strict chain of subspaces has strictly increasing dimensions. Its simple constituents are therefore well defined up to isomorphism and multiplicity.

<a id="stmt-prop-constituent-test"></a>
<div id="prop:constituent-test" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Constituents forced by finite assembly). *Suppose that $L_{K(2)}X$ belongs to the ordinary thick subcategory generated by $\mathbf 1_2$ in $K(2)$-local spectra. Then, for every $d\in\mathbb Z$, the representation $\mathcal V_d(X)$ is finite-dimensional over $Q_x$, and each simple constituent is isomorphic to $\overline\omega^{\otimes b}$ for some $b\in\mathbb Z$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-constituent-test"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-constituent-test</summary>

*Proof.* Let $\mathcal A$ be the full subcategory of finite-dimensional semilinear $G_F$-representations over $Q_x$ whose simple constituents have the indicated form. It is a Serre subcategory: subobjects and quotients retain their constituents, and the constituents of an extension are those of its two ends. It is also closed under finite sums and retracts.

The functor $\mathcal T$ is exact. Localization of modules from $\mathcal O_x$ to $Q_x$ is exact and respects the semilinear action. An exact triangle $X_1\to X_2\to X_3$ consequently gives an exact sequence $$\mathcal V_d(X_1)\longrightarrow\mathcal V_d(X_2)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal V_d(X_3)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal V_{d-1}(X_1)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal V_{d-1}(X_2).$$ If the terms arising from $X_1$ and $X_2$ lie in $\mathcal A$ in every degree, then $\mathcal V_d(X_3)$ is an extension of a subobject of $\mathcal V_{d-1}(X_1)$ by a quotient of $\mathcal V_d(X_2)$, and hence also lies in $\mathcal A$. Rotation gives the same assertion for any choice of two terms. The condition is therefore closed under finite sums, shifts, cofibers, and retracts.

For the local unit, $\mathcal T(\mathbf 1_2)\simeq E_2/p$. Indeed, $S\to\mathbf 1_2$ is a $K(2)$-equivalence, tensoring with $E_2$ preserves $K(2)$-equivalences, and $E_2$ is $K(2)$-local. Even periodicity gives $$\mathcal V_{2j}(\mathbf 1_2)=\overline\omega^{\otimes j},
 \qquad \mathcal V_{2j+1}(\mathbf 1_2)=0.$$ The same observation shows that $\mathcal T(X)\simeq\mathcal T(L_{K(2)}X)$ for every $X$. The condition therefore holds on every object of the ordinary thick subcategory generated by $\mathbf 1_2$, and hence on $X$ as stated. ◻

</details>

The Proposition proves finite dimensionality as well as the restriction on constituents. It uses neither closure under tensoring with arbitrary modules nor an identification of dualizable objects with objects in the ordinary thick subcategory. To contradict it, the rest of the argument will exhibit one actual simple subquotient that is not a periodicity power.

<a id="sec:setup"></a>
### Markings, coefficients, and conventions

The detector in Section <a href="#sec:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:detector">2</a> gives a condition that any finite construction must satisfy. We now prepare the coefficient calculation that will violate it. The prime remains $p=3$, and $k$ is the finite field chosen there, containing $\mathbb F_{p^6}$ and enlarged finitely when needed to fix the constant identifications. Recall the Honda groups $\Gamma_i$, their division algebras $D_i$, and their stabilizers $P_i=\mathcal O_{D_i}^{\times}$. The Honda forms can be chosen over $\mathbb F_p$, and their endomorphisms are defined over $\mathbb F_{p^i}$. Thus $k$ contains both endomorphism fields needed in heights two and three. Set <a id="eq:stabilizers"></a>$$\label{eq:stabilizers}
 P=P_3,\qquad
 H=\ker(\mathop{\mathrm{Nrd}}:P\longrightarrow\mathbb Z_p^\times),\qquad
 J=P_2.$$ The actions below are the unextended stabilizer actions: they fix $k$. When Galois descent is involved, it will be handled explicitly rather than included silently in $P$ or $J$.

Choose the unramified quadratic subfield $F\subset D_2$. The character used in the obstruction is <a id="eq:norm-character"></a>$$\label{eq:norm-character}
 \delta:\mathcal O_F^\times\longrightarrow\mathbb F_3^\times,
 \qquad
 \delta(g)=\overline{N_{F/\mathbb Q_3}(g)}.$$ It is nontrivial because the residue-field norm $\mathbb F_9^\times\to\mathbb F_3^\times$ is surjective. Its inverse and its unramified Galois conjugate are equal to it.

We also use $$H'=\mathop{\mathrm{Nrd}}^{-1}(\mu_2),\qquad
 H'/H=\mu_2,\qquad
 P/H'\cong 1+3\mathbb Z_3\cong\mathbb Z_3.$$ Here the reduced norm on maximal-order units is surjective. For these particular algebras, one may see this on the units of an unramified maximal subfield: the residue norm is surjective, and the norm on principal units is surjective by the trace and the $3$-adic logarithm. No group section of the full reduced norm is needed.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### The one-parameter stratum

We first fix the coordinate normalization behind the Hasse coefficients. Lubin–Tate construct a universal deformation from a normalized representative of the special fibre (Lubin and Tate 1966, Proposition 1.1, p. 50, and Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[LT66](#ref-LT66)]. On the stratum where the preceding parameters vanish, their parameter $\tau_r$, with $q=p^r$, occurs as $$\Phi(X,Y)\equiv X+Y+\tau_r C_q(X,Y)
       \pmod{\text{total degree }q+1},
 \qquad
 C_q(X,Y)=\frac{(X+Y)^q-X^q-Y^q}{p}.$$ The expression defining $C_q$ is the integral polynomial used in Lazard (1955, equations (2.4)–(2.5), p. 256) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Lazard55](#ref-Lazard55)]. Iterating the group law $p$ times and reducing in characteristic $p$ gives $$\begin{split}
 [p]_{\Phi}(T)
 &\equiv \tau_r\sum_{j=1}^{p-1} C_q(jT,T)\\
 &=\tau_r\frac{p^q-p}{p}T^q
  \equiv-\tau_r T^q\pmod{T^{q+1}}.
 \end{split}$$ Thus the raw successive Hasse coefficient in that normalized representative is $-\tau_r$.

The Honda coordinate was already fixed in Section <a href="#sec:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:detector">2</a>. Choose the special-fibre coordinate isomorphism from the normalized representative to that Honda law and transport the universal family along it. Write $\lambda_h\in k^\times$ for its linear coefficient in height $h$. The normalization preceding Lubin–Tate Proposition 1.1 uses an isomorphism over the original residue field. Since the height-two Honda law is defined over $\mathbb F_9$, we perform that construction already there, so $\lambda_2\in\mathbb F_9^\times$. This transport makes the special fibre literally the chosen Honda law, so its $p$-series is still $T^{p^h}$. Under a coordinate change $T'=cT+O(T^2)$, the first nonzero coefficient at $T^{p^r}$ becomes $c^{1-p^r}$ times the old coefficient. We therefore name the height-three parameters $$a=-\lambda_3^{1-p}\tau_1,\qquad
 t=-\lambda_3^{1-p^2}\tau_2,$$ and name the height-two parameter $x=-\lambda_2^{1-p}\tau_1$. These are unit rescalings of regular parameters. In the transported families the first raw Hasse coefficient is exactly $a$, the next one modulo $a$ is exactly $t$, and the first height-two coefficient is exactly $x$. These are chosen coordinate normalizations, rather than identities independent of a coordinate. In the Lubin–Tate representative itself they read $a=-\tau_1$, $t=-\tau_2$, and $x=-\tau_1$.

We choose the mixed-characteristic height-two parameter compatibly. Lift the chosen height-two coordinate isomorphism coefficientwise to $W(\mathbb F_9)$; its linear coefficient remains a unit. Transport the mixed-characteristic Lubin–Tate family along this lift. The resulting family over $W(\mathbb F_9)[[\tau_1]]$ has a regular parameter lifting this $x$: multiply $\tau_1$ by a lift of the nonzero constant $-\lambda_2^{1-p}$. We denote the lift by $x$ as well and use the scalar-extended family over $W(k)[[x]]$ for $E_2(k)$. Functorial Morava $E$-theory realization (Goerss and Hopkins 2004, sec. 7 and Corollary 7.7) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[GH04](#ref-GH04)] supplies the map from $E_2(\mathbb F_9)$ to $E_2(k)$; its coefficient map sends the chosen regular parameter $x$ to $x$. This convention will be used in the completion comparison in Section <a href="#sec:homotopy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:homotopy">8</a>.

Let $$A=k[[a,t]]$$ be the universal equal-characteristic deformation ring of $\Gamma_3$ in this normalization (Lubin and Tate 1966, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[LT66](#ref-LT66)]. Let $\mathcal G$ denote the associated algebraic $p$-divisible group over $A$.

We specify the order of construction, since it matters after $t$ is inverted. The formal multiplication-by-$p^r$ series over $A$ is distinguished of degree $p^{3r}$. Weierstrass preparation gives its finite flat kernel over $A$; the formal group law supplies its group structure. These kernels, with their compatible inclusions, form $\mathcal G$. This is the finite-torsion construction over a complete local base in Tate (1967, sec. 2.2, Proposition 1) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[Tate67](#ref-Tate67)]. This equivalence also transfers universality to the algebraic family. Let $B$ be a complete Noetherian local $k$-algebra with residue field $k$, and let $H/B$ be a marked deformation of $\Gamma_3[p^\infty]$. For $Q_r=\mathcal O(H[p^r])$, the algebra $$Q_r/\mathfrak m_BQ_r\cong k[T]/(T^{p^{3r}})$$ is local. Since $Q_r$ is finite over $B$, every maximal ideal lies over $\mathfrak m_B$, so $Q_r$ is local and $H$ is connected in Tate’s sense. The inverse equivalence gives a formal group of dimension one, as on the closed fibre; a coordinate lifting the marking makes it a Lubin–Tate deformation. Full faithfulness identifies its marked isomorphisms (Tate 1967, sec. 2.2, Proposition 1, and Section 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Tate67](#ref-Tate67)]. The prepared finite kernels commute with local base change. Hence Lubin–Tate universality transfers to the above algebraic $\mathcal G/A$, supplying the universality used by the later Kodaira–Spencer argument (Lubin and Tate 1966, Theorem 3.1 and paragraph 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[LT66](#ref-LT66)]. We then pull this finite torsion system back along $$A\longrightarrow K=k((t)),\qquad a\longmapsto0.$$ On this stratum the $p$-series factors through the $p^2$-power Frobenius, and the remaining homomorphism has invertible linear coefficient $t$. Thus $\mathcal G_K$ has a connected part of height two and an étale quotient of height one. Forming only the formal completion at the identity after this base change would discard the latter quotient.

The ring-theoretic differential statement needed later is elementary in these coordinates.

<a id="stmt-lem-finite-p-basis"></a>
<div id="lem:finite-p-basis" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *The ordinary module $\Omega^1_{A/k}$ is free on $da,dt$ and agrees with the module of continuous differentials. The element $t$ is a $p$-basis of $K$, remains a $p$-basis at each finite separable field extension of $K$, and gives $\Omega^1_{L/k}=L\,dt$ for the marking algebra defined below.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-finite-p-basis"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-finite-p-basis</summary>

*Proof.* Since $k$ is perfect, every $f\in A$ has a unique expansion $$f=\sum_{0\leq i,j<p}a^it^j f_{ij}^{\,p},
          \qquad f_{ij}\in A.$$ Ordinary derivations therefore have the usual partial-derivative formula and are determined freely by the values of $a,t$. The continuous partial derivatives show that these values are independent. This proves the assertion for $A$. The corresponding one-variable expansion proves the assertion for $K$. A $p$-basis is preserved by a separable algebraic extension: Frobenius base change along a separable extension is an isomorphism, so the basis of $K$ over $K^p$ remains a basis after that base change. The differential statement follows by the same argument. For a finite étale algebra it holds on each field factor, and it passes to the filtered union defining $L$. ◻

</details>

The universal deformation theory of $p$-divisible groups gives a functorial Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism over $A$ (Lau 2010, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[Lau10](#ref-Lau10)]; the version over arbitrary square-zero target rings is recalled where it is used in Proposition <a href="#prop:invariant-differential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:invariant-differential">33</a> and Theorem <a href="#thm:jet-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:jet-comparison">48</a>. Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-p-basis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-p-basis">4</a> explains why the ordinary algebraic differentials in that theorem are available after specializing to the Laurent field $K$.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### The connected marking tower

The connected-marking tower is the coheight-one construction studied by Gross and Torii (Gross 1979, Theorem 3.5(1a)) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[Gross79](#ref-Gross79)] <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[Torii03](#ref-Torii03)] (Torii 2003, sec. 2.3 and Theorem 2.9). In Torii’s Laurent-field setting the lower-height stabilizer acts simply transitively on markings and commutes with the higher-height action. We use the ind-étale torsor form, allowing products of fields at finite stages, and prove the integral étale marking and finite-level descent needed below.

Let $L$ be the algebra classifying isomorphisms between the connected formal law of $\mathcal G_K$ and the constant Honda formal group $\Gamma_2$. Its coefficient construction and separability are proved in Section <a href="#sec:towers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:towers">4</a>. The marking space is a pro-étale torsor under the pro-constant group $J$. We write <a id="eq:marking-algebra"></a>$$\label{eq:marking-algebra}
          L=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{U\subset J} L^U ,$$ where $U$ runs through open subgroups and $L^U$ is the finite étale $K$-algebra of markings modulo $U$. The superscript is compatible with the $J$-action on the tower. The finite stages need not be fields.

The height-three stabilizer $P$ acts on the universal deformation and preserves its height strata (Lubin and Tate 1966, Proposition 3.3 and paragraph 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[LT66](#ref-LT66)]. It also transports a connected marking. Changes of the connected marking give the commuting $J$-action. Thus $P$ preserves each $L^U$, while $J$ carries stages to the corresponding conjugate stages. The actions on finite jets and their étale lifts are continuous. Normalize the valuation of $K$ by $v(t)=1$, extend it to each field factor of $L^U$, and give a product of factors its product topology. The $P$-action preserves these valuations, since its action on the height-two stratum takes $t$ to a unit multiple of $t$.

For a finite stage $B=L^U$, put $$B_e=B^{1/p^e},\qquad
 B^{\mathrm{perf}}=\bigcup_{e\geq0}B_e,\qquad
 B^\flat=\widehat{B^{\mathrm{perf}}}.$$ Roots are taken inside the perfection and all valuations are extended from $B$. Each $B_e$ is complete. The symbol $B^\flat$ in this paper means the *completed perfection of this finite stage*; it does not denote the completion of the entire marking tower. The other perfection used throughout the proof is $$R=L^{\mathrm{perf}}
                          =\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{U,e}(L^U)^{1/p^e}.$$ These conventions are applied factorwise to products of fields.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### Continuous cochains at finite stages

We use inhomogeneous continuous group cochains. If a compact group $Q$ acts continuously on a complete coefficient module $M$, then $C^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,M)=
\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q^s,M)$, with the usual bar differential. An extra compact profinite parameter space $T$ means $$C^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,M;T)
       =\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(T\times Q^s,M);$$ the differential leaves $T$ fixed. The parameter space need not be metrizable.

<a id="stmt-def-stage-cochains"></a>
<div id="def:stage-cochains" class="definition">

**Definition 5** (Stage cochains). For $Q=P,H,H'$, or a closed subgroup of one of these groups, cochains with the incomplete coefficients $L$ and $R$ mean <a id="eq:stage-cochains"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 C^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,L;T)
   &:=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_U
       \operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{cts}}(T\times Q^s,L^U),
       \nonumber\\
 C^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(Q,R;T)
   &:=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{U,e}
